L452: ## 7. Risk and Risk Management
L453: ## 7.1. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Nonclinical Data
L454: The nonclinical data submitted to support the safety evaluation of oteseconazole included pharmacology (primary, secondary and safety pharmacology), pharmacokinetics (absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion with toxicokinetics for pivotal studies) and toxicology (repeat-dose, genotoxicity, carcinogenicity, reproductive toxicity) studies in rats (up to two years), dogs (up to 39 weeks), mice (up to 26 weeks) and rabbits (during pregnancy to evaluate embryo-fetal toxicity). The identified risks should be manageable in the indicated population.
L455: ## Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetics
L456: In vitro studies showed that oteseconazole inhibited sterol 14-demethylation in two strains of *Candida albicans* and almost completely blocked formation of ergosterol, leading to the
L457: accumulation of lanosterol and other toxic methylated steroids. In vitro binding studies showed oteseconazole inhibited binding most potently at the rat cerebral cortex Cl^{-} channel, the human adenosine A3 receptor, and the human melatonin MT1 receptors. Oteseconazole was also shown to inhibit the human efflux transporter breast cancer resistance protein with a half maximal inhibitory concentration (IC_{50}) of 0.4\muM. Oteseconazole administered as a single oral dose (up to 1000 mg/kg) had no significant effect on neurobehavioral or respiratory function in rats. Similarly, a single oral dose (up to 300 mg/kg) had no significant effect on cardiovascular function in dogs. In rats and dogs, oteseconazole exposure was less dose proportional and the drug accumulated up to 126-fold in dogs with repeated dosing over 39 weeks. The half-life was 30 days in dogs and 8.5 days in rats. In a tissue distribution study in rats, the highest levels of oteseconazole were observed in the small intestines and in the Harderian gland.
L458: ## Toxicology
L459: Reproductive and developmental toxicology studies included fertility studies in male and female rats, embryo-fetal development studies in rats and rabbits, and four pre- and postnatal development studies in rats. Although fertility was unaffected, increased incidences of abnormal sperm were observed at 3 mg/kg/day and sperm counts were reduced at 10 mg/kg/day. Fertility in female rats was comparable to that of controls after exposure to oteseconazole. There was no embryo-fetal toxicity or malformations at 40 mg/kg/day following administration of oteseconazole during organogenesis in pregnant rats. Abortions occurred in rabbits in the presence of maternal toxicity (reduced bodyweight gain with reduced food consumption), but there were no drug-related malformations at 15 mg/kg/day following administration of oteseconazole during organogenesis in pregnant rabbits. In pre- and postnatal studies, oteseconazole administration to pregnant and lactating rats at 7.5 mg/kg/day resulted in lens opacities, swollen eyes, hemorrhage, hyphema, bupthalmos, corneal ulceration, retinal and optic nerve atrophy, as well as lens degeneration. Opacities were consistently found regardless of the strain of rat tested.
L460: Oteseconazole was negative in the bacterial reverse mutation assay, in vitro chromosomal aberration assay, and in vivo micronucleus assay in rats.
L461: In a 2-year oral carcinogenicity study of oteseconazole, male Sprague Dawley rats showed increased incidence of Leydig cell adenomas of the testes and thyroid follicular cell adenomas/carcinomas combined at \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day. In females, thyroid follicular cell adenomas and carcinomas were increased at \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day and thyroid carcinomas were increased at 5/3 mg/kg/day. Hemorrhage was increased in several tissues in male rats, but fewer tissues were affected in female rats (details below).
L462: There was no increase in the incidence of tumors following daily oral administration of oteseconazole to Tg.rasH2 mice for 6 months at doses of 5 mg/kg/day in males and 15 mg/kg/day in females.
L463: There are no safety concerns regarding the observed or potential impurities in oteseconazole. The limit for each individual related product was set at \le 0.2\%, consistent with International Conference on Harmonisation (ICH) Q3B(R2) and was within applicable ICH M7(R1) limits based upon a maximum daily dose of 600 mg (June 2006; March 2017). All individual impurities have been detected at less than (b)(4) %. No (b)(4) were detected in the oteseconazole drug substance manufacturing process. The risk for (b)(4) formation appears to be very low.
L464: ## 7.2. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Drug Class or Other Drug-Specific Factors
L465: Oteseconazole is an azole antifungal. Well-known azole associated adverse reactions include hepatic injury/hepatotoxicity, arrhythmias and QT-prolongations, embryo-fetal toxicity, and hypersensitivity reactions. Adverse reactions that are not azole class specific but have been identified in at least one drug in this class include visual disturbances; photosensitivity; severe cutaneous reactions (e.g., Stevens-Johnson syndrome [SJS], toxic epidermal necrolysis [TEN], and drug reaction with eosinophilia and systemic symptoms [DRESS]); adrenal dysfunction (e.g., Cushing syndrome or adrenal insufficiency); fluorosis; and electrolyte imbalances. Azole class-associated treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) identified during review of oteseconazole are discussed in greater detail under Section 17.3 of this review.
L466: ## 7.3. Potential Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L467: There is no available postmarket experience with oteseconazole at this time.
L468: ## 7.4. FDA Approach to the Safety Review
L469: Three phase 3 trials, Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017, were most relevant to the safety review of oteseconazole. The phase 2b dose-finding study, Trial CL-006, provided additional support for oteseconazole safety. While not a focal point of the safety evaluation, oteseconazole safety was further characterized by review of TEAEs reported in the Applicant's phase 1 PK studies and non-RVVC phase 2a studies. These studies are also briefly discussed in this review.
L470: Using MAED, the clinical reviewer generated SMQ listings of well-documented adverse reactions associated with commonly used azole antifungals (e.g., fluconazole, voriconazole, posaconazole) including: hepatotoxicity, QT-prolongation and arrhythmia, phototoxicity/skin reactions, visual disturbances, and pancreatitis. Since hypersensitivity is a contraindication for any drug, CMQs/SMQs associated with hypersensitivity reactions were generated using Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities versions 21.0 (for Trials CL-011, -012, and -017) and 17.1 (for Trial CL-006).
L471: ## 7.5. Adequacy of Clinical Safety Database
L472: A total of 1,663 subjects were included in the 13 phase 1 to 3 trials the Applicant conducted in support of the clinical development of oteseconazole. Of these subjects, 1,655 comprised the Applicant's clinical safety development program. Eight subjects, all in the phase 2/3 trials, were enrolled but not dosed and were, therefore, not included in the overall safety population for any of several reasons (e.g., investigator discretion, withdrawal of consent, being lost to follow-up or a screen failure, etc.).
L473: The Applicant's clinical safety development program included 1,655 subjects who comprised the entire safety population, 1,221 of whom received at least one dose of oteseconazole and 434 of whom received at least one dose of placebo. The 1,221 subjects in the oteseconazole arm were enrolled in at least one of 13 clinical trials supportive of oteseconazole's development: four
L474: phase 1 single-dose trials in healthy volunteers (Trials CL-001, CL-007 [PKE cohort], CL-013, and CL-016); two phase 1 multiple-dose trials in healthy volunteers (Trials CL-002 and CL-008); two phase 2 trials comprised of non-RVVC subjects (Trials CL-003 and CL-005); a single phase 2a acute VVC trial (Trial CL-004); a single phase 2 dose-finding trial (Trial CL-006), and three phase 3 trials (Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017). Of the 1,082 subjects enrolled and randomized into the phase 2/3 RVVC studies, 746 subjects were assigned to receive oteseconazole and 336 subjects were assigned to receive placebo. A total of 621 subjects, all of whom were in the phase 3 trials and 41 of whom were in the phase 2 dose-ranging trial (CL-006), received the to-be-marketed oteseconazole dosing of a 1,050 mg loading dose (LD) and a 1,650 mg maintenance dose (MD).
L475: As stated above, the primary focus of this review was assessing oteseconazole safety in the three phase 3 trials (Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017), with a secondary focus on the phase 2 dose-finding trial (Trial CL-006) conducted in support of oteseconazole safety. The clinical reviewer considered the overall size of the oteseconazole safety database adequate.

L480: Mean and median durations of drug exposure, in weeks, in all phase 3 trials were mostly balanced across oteseconazole and placebo treatment arms, with most subjects remaining on the study arm over a median of 12 weeks in the pooled phase 3 analysis. A 12-week duration of exposure is consistent with the written protocols.
L481: Similarly, the percentage of patients remaining in Trials CL-011 and CL-012 was balanced across treatment arms, with approximately three-quarters or more of all subjects exposed to the study drug for \ge 12 to &lt;26 weeks. In Trial CL-017, while the duration of treatment across both the oteseconazole and placebo arms was balanced, with 64.4% and 66.7% of subjects receiving \ge 12 to &lt;26 weeks of treatment, respectively, the percentage of subjects in this trial was slightly less than the percentages in Trials CL-011 and CL-012.
L482: There was a noticeable difference between the oteseconazole and placebo arms in the phase 2b Trial CL-006, with the mean and median durations of exposure of 16.8 and 14.7 weeks, respectively, in the oteseconazole arm being considerably less than the mean and median durations of exposure in the placebo arm, 21.7 and 24, respectively. This discrepancy is likely because placebo subjects received treatment for 24 weeks, while oteseconazole subjects received 12 or 24 weeks of treatment. The majority of patients in this trial remained in the study from between \ge 12 to &lt;26 weeks, 85.5% and 88.9%, respectively.
L483: ## 7.6. Safety Findings and Concerns Based on Review of Clinical Safety Database
L484: ## 7.6.1. Overall Adverse Event Summaries
L485: ## Phase 3 Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017
L486: A tabular overview of all TEAEs occurring in the phase 3 trials is shown in Table 36 below. A total of 871 participants comprised the pooled phase 3 safety population: 580 in the oteseconazole arm and 291 in the placebo arm.
L487: The percentages of all TEAEs across the oteseconazole and the placebo arms were nearly equal, with the exception being Trial CL-017, in which a larger percentage of TEAEs occurred in the placebo arm than in the oteseconazole arm (63.9% in placebo versus 54.1% in oteseconazole). There were few SAEs in the three phase 3 trials, and the number of SAEs across both treatment arms were near balanced. A single death occurred among the 871 subjects of the pooled phase 3 safety population, Subject (b) (4), (b) (6), (b) (3) in the oteseconazole arm of Trial CL-017. This participant is discussed in greater detail below; however, the death was deemed to be unrelated to oteseconazole exposure.
L488: In the pooled phase 3 analysis, slightly more severe TEAEs occurred in subjects in the oteseconazole arm, whereas moderate and mild TEAEs were similar across the study arms. Few subjects experienced nonfatal moderate to severe TEAEs. This was most likely due to the disease entity being studied (RVVC) and the younger study population consisting of primarily young females (most of whom were between 18 to 34 years of age) who were generally healthy and with few co-morbid conditions.
L489: Similarly, few TEAEs leading to study discontinuation occurred in the pooled phase 3 safety population, with near equal percentages of subjects discontinuing study treatment across
L490: NDA 215888
Vivjoa (oteseconazole)
L491: treatment arms (0.7% versus 0.3%, respectively, in the pooled oteseconazole and placebo arms), and with slightly more discontinuations occurring among oteseconazole subjects (four subjects versus one subject).
L492: Irrespective of treatment arm, most TEAEs occurred in the Infections and infestations, Gastrointestinal disorders, Reproductive systems and breast disorders, and Nervous system SOCs. The reader is referred to Section 7.6.5 for more details.

L502: ## **Baseline Demographic Characteristics**
L503: The phase 3 safety population was similar to the ITT population. The majority of females in both the oteseconazole and placebo arms were White (75.0% oteseconazole versus 75.3% placebo), non-Latino females (84.8% versus 85.2%) with an overall mean age and mean baseline BMI of 34.5 and 26, respectively. The majority of females were either on hormonal contraceptives or had an inserted intrauterine device. Most females were from the United States (53.6% versus 51.9%), followed by females from European countries (39.5% versus 41.2%), most of whom were from Eastern Europe. Please refer to Table 169 in the Appendix for further details.
L504: ## **Past Medical History**
L505: Among the 871 subjects in the combined phase 3 trials, a total 653 (75.0%) subjects (429 oteseconazole subjects and 224 placebo subjects) were reported to have had prior medical conditions.
L506: The most common medical conditions among oteseconazole subjects included: anxiety/anxiety disorders and symptoms (PT: anxiety, anxiety disorder, generalized anxiety disorder) (78 subjects); seasonal allergy (72 subjects); caesarean section (65 subjects); headaches/migraines (PT: headache, migraine, migraine without aura, retinal migraine, tension headache) (64 subjects); depression/depressed mood disorders and disturbances (PT: depression, major depression, perinatal depression, persistent depressive disorder) (56 subjects); hypertension (52 subjects); drug hypersensitivity/allergic conditions (PT: drug hypersensitivity, hypersensitivity, rubber sensitivity) (48 subjects); asthma (37 subjects); gastroesophageal reflux disease (35 subjects); hysterectomy (35 subjects); female sterilization (32 subjects); hypothyroidism (32 subjects).
L507: Among placebo subjects, the most common prior medical conditions included: headaches (PT: headache, migraine, migraine with aura, migraine without aura, post-traumatic headache, sinus headache, tension headache) (35 subjects); caesarean section (31 subjects); seasonal allergy (31 subjects); anxiety/anxiety disorders and symptoms (PT: anxiety, anxiety disorder) (27 subjects); depression/depressed mood disorders and disturbances (PT: depression, depressed mood) (26 subjects); hypertension (PT: hypertension, essential hypertension) (25 subjects); drug hypersensitivity/allergic conditions (PT: drug hypersensitivity, hypersensitivity, multiple allergies, rubber sensitivity) (24 subjects); female sterilization (20 subjects); hysterectomy (20 subjects); gastroesophageal reflux disease (19 subjects).
L508: ## **Phase 2 Trial CL-006**
L509: The phase 2b safety population was comprised of 211 subjects: 166 subjects in the combined oteseconazole arms and 45 subjects in the placebo arm. The 166 oteseconazole subjects were subdivided into 4 treatment arms which included: 41 subjects in the 150 mg x 12 weeks arm; 42 subjects in the 150 mg x 24 weeks arm; 42 subjects in the 300 mg x 12 weeks arm; and 41 subjects in the 150 mg x 24 weeks arm.
L510: A summary of all Trial CL-006 TEAEs is provided below. There were a total of 447 TEAEs nearly evenly distributed across all five treatment arms of Trial CL-006, with noticeably fewer numbers of TEAEs in the 300 mg x 24 weeks (n=77 TEAEs) dose arm. Otherwise, there were 95, 88, and 95 TEAEs occurring in the oteseconazole 150 mg x 12 weeks, 150 mg x 24 weeks, 300 mg x 12 weeks arms, respectively, and 92 TEAEs in the placebo.
L511: No fatal serious adverse events (SAEs) occurred among any subjects in Trial CL-006. There was a total of five nonfatal SAEs in the combined oteseconazole arm, two of which occurred in the same subject (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6)—PTs: abdominal pain and acute pancreatitis) compared with one subject who experienced a single nonfatal SAE in the placebo arm (PT: appendicitis). There were only two subjects, both from the oteseconazole arm, who prematurely discontinued the study drug, with one of these TEAEs being an SAE (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) PT: dermatitis allergic; and Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) PT: Pancreatitis [SAE]). Most TEAEs were either mild or moderate and occurred in near similar percentages across all treatment arms. However, the fewest number of mild TEAEs occurred in the oteseconazole 300 mg x 24 weeks arm, and the highest number of severe TEAEs occurred in this treatment arm as well.

L518: ## **Baseline Demographic Characteristics and Past Medical History**
L519: The safety population of Trial CL-006 was comprised mostly of White, non-Latino females with an overall mean age and mean baseline BMI of 34.8 and 27.4, respectively. The most common medical conditions found among participants randomized to oteseconazole included bacterial vaginosis, depression, obesity, and anxiety, whereas in the placebo arm, bacterial vaginosis was the most common medical condition. A summary of baseline demographic features of subjects in Trial CL-006 is found in Section 16.5 in the Appendix.
L520: ## **7.6.2. Deaths**
L521: ## **Phase 3 Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017**
L522: A single treatment-emergent death (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6)) occurred in the pooled phase 3 trials. Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) was a 50-year-old female who upon enrolling into Trial CL-017 was randomized to receive oteseconazole treatment. The subject's death from COVID-19-associated acute respiratory failure is described in detail below, and was assessed by the Applicant, the Study Investigator and the clinical reviewer as being unrelated to oteseconazole. In addition, this was the single reported death to occur in any treatment arm among the major phase 2-3 trials conducted in support of oteseconazole's clinical development, which is largely reflective of the disease entity, RVVC, and the target population which was primarily generally healthy young females.
L523: ## **Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) (Oteseconazole)**
L524: *(Fatal SAE PT: Acute respiratory failure/Nonfatal SAE PT: Pneumonia viral)*
L525: A 50-year-old White, non-Latino American female with a prior medical history most noted for Sjögren's syndrome (since [REDACTED](b)(6)), treated with hydroxychloroquine (Plaquenil) and cevimeline HCl (Evoxac; for treatment of symptoms of dry mouth in patients with Sjögren's syndrome); hypertension (since [REDACTED](b)(6)); obesity (BMI 31.3); depression (since [REDACTED](b)(6)); and a history of RVVC, with three prior episodes of acute VVC within the 12 months prior to study enrollment.
L526: On Study Day 279, the subject was diagnosed with COVID-19 pneumonia (nonfatal PT: pneumonia viral), for which the subject was hospitalized. According to the Applicant, limited details on hospitalization were available. Action taken with study drug was listed as “new drug therapy added and discontinued from study.” The subject was reported to have developed a fatal SAE on Study Day 305 (PT: acute respiratory failure) due to COVID-19. Subject's date of death and final day in the study were the same, Study Day 305.
L527: *Study Investigator's/Applicant's Impression:* The study investigator and the Applicant assessed the event (PTs: pneumonia viral [COVID-19] and acute respiratory failure) as being unrelated to oteseconazole exposure.
L528: ***Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:*** *On review of the accompanying case narrative and clinical data, the reviewer agrees with the Study Investigator and Applicant that the fatal SAE (PT: acute respiratory failure) was unrelated to oteseconazole but was instead due to infection with COVID-19 in a person who was immunocompromised with a rheumatologic condition, Sjögren's syndrome, and with a BMI of 31.3.*
L529: ## Phase 2b Trial CL-006
L530: No fatal events occurred in Trial CL-006.
L531: ## 7.6.3. Nonfatal Serious Adverse Events
L532: ## Phase 3 Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017
L533: A total of 35 nonfatal treatment-emergent SAEs occurred in 22 (22/871; 2.5%) subjects during the phase 3 trials: 12 events in CL-011, 15 events in CL-012, and 8 events in CL-017. Eighteen nonfatal SAEs occurred in 13 subjects (13/580; 2.2%) randomized to the oteseconazole arms, (including Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) [in CL-011] and Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) [in CL-017] with 2 and 5 nonfatal SAEs, respectively), and 17 nonfatal SAE events occurred in 9 subjects (9/291; 3.1%) receiving placebo, including two subjects each with more than one nonfatal SAE (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) with six nonfatal SAEs in CL-011 and Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) in CL-012 with four separate nonfatal SAEs all coded to PT depression).
L534: All nonfatal SAEs in the oteseconazole arm were deemed either unrelated to or unlikely to be related to the study drug by the Applicant and Study Investigators. All but two resulted in the resolution of the recorded events, and none required a change in oteseconazole dosing. Four nonfatal SAEs in the oteseconazole arm occurred while on study drug, and the other 14 events occurred while subjects remained enrolled in the study but were no longer receiving study drug. The clinical reviewer conducted a detailed review of all phase 3 nonfatal SAEs and agrees with the Applicant and Study Investigators that no nonfatal SAEs occurring in the oteseconazole arm were attributable to oteseconazole exposure.
L535: Most nonfatal SAEs occurring in the placebo arm occurred under the Infections and infestations system organ class (SOC), in a total of five subjects with six documented nonfatal SAEs. Three nonfatal SAEs in the placebo arm occurred while the subjects were on treatment, while all other treatment-emergent nonfatal SAEs occurred while the subjects remained in the study but were no longer receiving treatment. All nonfatal SAEs in the placebo arm were considered unrelated to study treatment by the Study Investigator and Applicant; all but one recovered from the event and no subjects required a change in study drug dosing (with data missing for one patient).
L536: Refer to Section 17.1 in the Appendix for narratives of all the pooled phase 3 nonfatal SAEs.

L561: ## 7.6.4. Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Events
L562: ## Phase 3 Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017
L563: Five subjects (5/871; 0.57%) in the pooled phase 3 trials, four of whom were randomized to the oteseconazole arm (4/580; 0.70%) and one randomized to placebo (1/291; 0.34%), had TEAEs resulting in study drug discontinuation. Three of the four events occurring in the oteseconazole arm were assessed by the Study Investigator as being related (Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6)/PT: dermatitis allergic) or possibly related to oteseconazole (Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6)/PT: abdominal distension and Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6)/PT: blister); whereas one TEAE in the oteseconazole arm resulting in study drug discontinuation was assessed as being unrelated or unlikely to be related (Subject: [REDACTED] (b) (6)
L564: (b) (6)/PT: vaginal infection) to the study drug. All subjects were reported to have recovered from the event, with the exception of one subject for whom the event outcome was missing. A single event was reported to have occurred while the subject was not actively receiving treatment (Subject (b) (6)/PT: influenza). All other subjects had been receiving study treatment at the time of the event.
L565: Upon review of all phase 3 treatment-emergent adverse events resulting in oteseconazole discontinuation, the clinical reviewer made the following determinations: PT: vaginal infection (Subject (b) (6)) was deemed a potential treatment failure; PT: blister (Subject (b) (6)) had insufficient evidence to definitively exclude the possibility that the observed event was unrelated to oteseconazole exposure; PT: abdominal distention was determined to be possibly related to oteseconazole exposure; and PT: allergic dermatitis (Subject (b) (6)) was deemed to be definitely related to oteseconazole exposure.
L566: Refer to Section 17.2 of the Appendix for narratives of all the pooled phase 3 treatment-emergent adverse events leading to discontinuation of study drug.

L576: ## Phase 2b CL-006
L577: A total of two TEAEs leading to discontinuation of the study drug occurred in Trial CL-006, both of which occurred in the oteseconazole arm: Subject (b)(6) (PT: dermatitis allergic) and Subject (b)(6) in the oteseconazole 150 mg x 12 weeks arm (PT: pancreatitis), which was also described above as a nonfatal SAE. No TEAEs leading to study discontinuation occurred in the placebo arm of Trial CL-006. Refer to Section 17.2.4 of the Appendix for further details on these subject narratives.
L578: ## 7.6.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
L579: ## Phase 3 Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017
L580: A total of 1,524 TEAEs (irrespective of causality) were reported in over half (56.5%; 492/871) of all subjects comprising the safety population: 55.2% (320/580) and 59.1% (172/291) in the oteseconazole and the placebo arms, respectively. The greatest numerical incidence of TEAEs in the pooled phase 3 safety population occurred under the Infections and infestations SOC (399 TEAEs) (36.6%, 212/580); followed by the Gastrointestinal disorders SOC (106 TEAEs) (12.4%, 71/580); the Reproductive system and breast disorders SOC (80 TEAEs) (10.2%, 59/580); the Nervous system disorders SOC (78 TEAEs) (9.8%, 57/580); the Investigations SOC (44 TEAEs) and the Musculoskeletal and connective tissue SOC (40 TEAEs) (6.0%, 35/580).
L581: The most common TEAEs in the oteseconazole arm were as follows: nasopharyngitis (12.8%; 74/580); urinary tract infections (8.8%, 51/580) (PT: urinary tract infection, cystitis, *Escherichia* urinary tract infection); bacterial vaginosis (PT: bacterial vaginosis, bacterial vulvovaginitis, and vaginitis *Gardnerella*) (7.6%, 47/580); headache (headache, migraines, and sinus headache) (7.4%; 43/580); and abdominal pain (abdominal discomfort, abdominal pain, abdominal pain lower, abdominal pain upper, and abdominal tenderness) (2.1%; 18/580). Likewise, in the placebo arm, the majority of TEAEs were coded under the Infections and infestations SOC, followed by the Reproductive system and breast disorders SOC; Gastrointestinal disorders SOC; Nervous system disorders SOC; and Musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders SOC. Upon evaluation of all TEAEs in the oteseconazole arm, the reviewer considered the following to be adverse reactions: urinary tract infections, bacterial vaginosis, headache, nausea, abdominal pain, pyrexia, and diarrhea.
L582: The clinical reviewer evaluated several TEAEs to determine if there were increased observations of VVC failures among subjects in both treatment arms with TEAEs coded to bacterial vaginosis (BV), urinary tract infection (UTI), STI (gonorrhea, chlamydia, genital herpes), which include signs and symptoms of vaginal irritation. None of the 4 subjects in the oteseconazole arm with a TEAE of VVC were confirmed-treatment failures; whereas, 5 of the 10 subjects in the placebo arm with TEAEs coded to the PT: vulvovaginal candidiasis and vulvovaginal mycotic infection were confirmed treatment failures.

L596: ## Phase 2b Trial CL-006
L597: Trial CL-006 was a dose range-finding multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled parallel-group efficacy, safety, tolerability and PK trial in which eligible prospective participants were randomized into placebo or one of four oteseconazole treatment arms.
L598: A total of 215 participants comprised the ITT population, four of whom were not included in the safety population of 211 subjects and did not receive study drug.
L599: A tabular summary (Table 170) of the phase 2b safety findings are described in the Appendix. The safety profile in Trial CL-006 reflects and supports the safety profile demonstrated in the combined phase 3 trials, where the most common TEAEs in the oteseconazole arm were found under the Infections and infestations SOC (e.g., BV, UTI, nasopharyngitis, and sinusitis); the Gastrointestinal disorders SOC (e.g., nausea, abdominal pain, abdominal distension, diarrhea); and the Reproductive system and breast disorders SOC (vulvovaginal irritation, menorrhagia/metrorrhagia).

The same is true of the key hematologic parameters in the supportive phase 2b trial (Trial CL-006).

L616: ## Creatine Phosphokinase
L617: An imbalance in CPK elevations was observed between subjects treated with oteseconazole when compared with those receiving placebo in the phase 3 and supportive phase 2 trials. It is noted that a 2:1 randomization ratio was used in the three phase 3 trials (Trials CL-011, CL-012, and CL-017), whereas a 1:1:1:1:1 randomization scheme was used for the phase 2b (Trial CL-006) (4 dosing cohorts received oteseconazole and a single cohort received placebo).
L618: The clinical reviewer used the DAIDS toxicity grading table to detect any significant maximal post-baseline changes in CPK values compared with baseline CPK values among the 561 and the 275 randomized subjects in the oteseconazole and placebo arms, respectively, with baseline and postbaseline CPK values in the combined phase 3 trials. The analysis identified 6 of 561 (1.1%) versus 1 of 275 subjects (0.4%), respectively, with normal baseline CPK (Grade 0) values who developed Grade 3 (severe) maximal post-baseline CPK elevations of 10 to &lt;20 x upper limit of normal (ULN) (with the ULN being 192 U/L). In addition, there were a total of 5 of 561 (0.9%) oteseconazole subjects versus 1 of 275 (0.4%) placebo subjects, respectively, with normal baseline CPK values who developed Grade 4 (potentially life-threatening) post-baseline CPK elevations of \ge 20 x ULN (Table 44). In most instances, the Applicant did not identify the observed maximal post-baseline CPK elevations as TEAEs. Narratives of these subjects can be found in Section 17.4 in the Appendix, and a more detailed discussion of the observed CPK elevations can be found under Section 7.7.2 Key Risk Review Issues.
L619: In light of the relationship between CPK elevations (specifically rhabdomyolysis) and such electrolyte disorders as hypokalemia or hyperkalemia, hypophosphatemia, and hypocalcemia, the reviewer directed special attention towards the evaluation of potassium (K^+), phosphate, and calcium (Ca^{2+}) and assessed maximal/minimal changes from baseline for these laboratory parameters. Upon investigation, no notable electrolyte imbalances between the oteseconazole arm or the placebo arm were appreciated. Importantly, there were no observed baseline imbalances of hypokalemia, hypophosphatemia, or hypocalcemia that could explain the observed CPK elevations. There were also no elevations in the lab parameters associated with observed CPK elevations (e.g., creatinine)

L631: As illustrated in the table above, in the smaller phase 2b study, CL-006, a single subject in the oteseconazole 1050 mg/150 mg x 11 weeks arm with a normal baseline CPK value developed Grade 3, 10 to &lt;20x ULN (1/160, 0.6%) maximal post-baseline CPK elevation and 5 out of a total 160 (3.1%) subjects across 3 of the 4 oteseconazole arms developed Grade 4 (\ge20x ULN) maximal post-baseline CPK values. No subjects in the placebo arm demonstrated either Grade 3 or Grade 4 CPK elevations. Narratives of these subjects can be found in Section 17.4 of the Appendix and a more detailed discussion of the observed CPK elevations can be found under Section 7.7.2.
L632: ## **Hepatic Function Tests**
L633: Hepatic injury, transaminase elevations, and cholestasis are well-known azole class-associated adverse reactions. As such, the reviewer evaluated graded maximal post baseline analyses for the transaminases; alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and aspartate aminotransferase (AST). Analysis of specific transaminases did not identify any signals indicative of hepatic failure. However, a consistent pattern of parallel, transient AST elevations appeared to emerge when evaluating transaminase elevations in the context of CPK elevations. Concomitant elevations in ALT were not consistently observed in subjects with CPK elevations. Shift tables generated to evaluate maximal post-baseline changes for total and direct bilirubin demonstrated no concerning safety signals to suggest the potential for cholestatic injury.
L634: Further discussion on increased transaminases co-occurring with CPK elevations is found separately under Section 7.7.2. Subject narratives can be found in Sections 17.3.1 and 17.4 in the Appendix.

L653: ## Phase 2b Trial CL-006
L654: There were no significant laboratory differences observed in the phase 2b Trial CL-006 than those observed in the combined phase 3 trials. However, a shift table of maximal post-baseline CPK changes relative to baseline values is found below.
L655: As illustrated in the table, a single subject receiving oteseconazole (150 mg x 3 months) with a Grade 0 (normal) baseline CPK value developed a Grade 3 maximal post-baseline CPK elevation (10 to &lt; 20 x ULN) versus no such subjects in the placebo arm. Similarly, there were five oteseconazole treated subjects with Grade 0 (normal) CPK baseline values who developed Grade 4 (\ge 20 \times \text{ULN}) maximal post-baseline CPK values: 1 in the oteseconazole 150 mg x 3 months arm; 3 in the oteseconazole 300-mg x 3 months; and 1 in the oteseconazole 300 mg x 6 months arm versus no subjects in the placebo arm or in the oteseconazole 150-mg x 6 months arm. The Applicant has suggested that the observed imbalance is attributable to differences in the randomization scheme. However, while there were no Grade 3 or 4 CPK elevations in the placebo arm, there were a total of 6 in the oteseconazole exposed subjects, with 4 of these 6 occurring in the 300-mg arm. This suggests that at the higher 300-mg dose there may have been a more pronounced dose correlation between oteseconazole and CPK laboratory elevations.
L656: Narratives of subjects with observed CPK elevations in Trial CL-006 can be found in Sections 7.7.2 and 17.4.4 of the Appendix.

L665: ## 7.6.7. Additional Safety Analyses
L666: ## 7.6.7.1. Adverse Events of Special Interest
L667: ## Hepatotoxicity—Evaluation for Hy's Law and Temple's Corollary Cases
L668: The review team performed an analysis to identify any potential Hy's law and/or Temple's corollary cases and to assess the potential for oteseconazole-induced hepatic injury. While no subjects in the pooled phase 3 or phase 2b trials fulfilled Hy's law criteria, there were, however, 10 subjects in the oteseconazole arm and 3 in the placebo arm who appeared to meet criteria for Temple's corollary, defined as having a maximum postbaseline total bilirubin less than 2x ULN and having a maximum postbaseline ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3x ULN. Transaminase elevations observed to be 10x – 15x ULN were most often associated with CPK elevations.
L669: **Figure 5. Drug-Induced Liver Injury Case Screening Plot, Safety Population, Trials 017, 012, 011 and 006 (ISS)***
L670: Source: adlb.xpt; Software: R
Each data point represents at least one visit (from a patient) with both ALT/AST and total bilirubin values in the postbaseline period. A potential Hy's Law case was defined as having a maximum postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2X ULN within 30 days after maximum postbaseline ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3X ULN, without findings of cholestasis (defined as ALP &lt;2X ULN). A Temple's Corollary case was defined as having a maximum postbaseline total bilirubin less than 2X ULN and maximum postbaseline ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3X ULN.
L671: * Reference Figure 9, page 101 of the STF report

L677: ## QT-Prolongation
L678: Azole class drugs (e.g., fluconazole, voriconazole, and posaconazole) have been causally associated with QT prolongation, torsades de pointes and/or arrhythmias.
L679: The table below summarizes the change in maximal post-baseline QTcF values among all participants with available baseline and postbaseline EKGs to identify any subjects with oteseconazole induced QTcF interval prolongations of &gt;480 msec - ≤500 msec and &gt;500 msec.
