L1676: ## **15.2. Trial CL-017 Trial Design Synopsis**
L1677: A synopsis for Trial VMT-VT-1161-CL-017 (CL-017) adapted from protocol synopsis version 2 (dated January 17, 2019).
L1678: APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL
L1679: ## PROTOCOL SYNOPSIS
L1680: Title of Study:	A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-Blind Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of VT-1161 Oral Capsules versus Fluconazole and Placebo in the Treatment of Acute Vulvovaginal Candidiasis Episodes in Subjects with Recurrent Vulvovaginal Candidiasis
Protocol Number:	VMT-VT-1161-CL-017
Interim Analysis:	No
Investigational Medicinal Product:	VT-1161 capsules
Non-Investigational Medicinal Product:	Fluconazole tablets
Planned Number of Sites:	Approximately 45 U.S.
Planned Number of Subjects:	360 screened; 180 randomized
Phase of Development:	3
Introduction:	
VT-1161 is an orally-available inhibitor of fungal CYP51 that has shown high potency and selectivity in in vitro studies. VT-1161 blocks the production of ergosterol, an essential component of the fungal cell membrane, which is critical to fungal growth. In vitro and in vivo pharmacology studies have demonstrated that VT-1161 is highly active against Candida albicans, including fluconazole-resistant C. albicans, and non-albicans Candida species that cause vulvovaginal candidiasis (VVC). VT-1161 is highly selective for fungal CYP51 relative to off-target human CYP enzymes, and data to date suggest that it may avoid the side-effect profile that limits the use of commonly prescribed azole antifungal agents.
Oral VT-1161 has been evaluated in Phase 1 and 2 studies and shown to be safe, well tolerated, and highly effective in the treatment of vulvovaginal infections caused by Candida species. For example, in a Phase 2b study in subjects with recurrent VVC (RVVC), subjects in the 12-week VT-1161 treatment group (150 mg VT-1161 once daily for 7 days followed by 150 mg VT-1161 once weekly for 11 weeks) showed a 4.8% recurrence rate through Week 48 compared with a 52% recurrence rate in the placebo group in the intent-to-treat analysis.
RVVC, defined as 3 or more acute VVC episodes over a 12-month period, is experienced by 5 to 7 million women each year in the US. Candida albicans is the leading cause of recurrent vulvovaginal yeast infections in the United States. However, there has been an increasing trend in the number of vaginal yeast infections attributable to non-albicans species such as C. glabrata and C. tropicalis. In addition to the use of topical products to treat VVC, the majority of the women visiting healthcare professionals receive fluconazole for the treatment of each acute episode. A smaller percentage are prescribed a fluconazole maintenance regimen although fluconazole is not currently approved for the treatment of RVVC. Since some of the C. albicans and the majority of non-albicans isolates are resistant to fluconazole, the management of RVVC has become even more challenging.
In both nonclinical studies and clinical trials conducted to date, VT-1161 has demonstrated a favorable safety and tolerability profile, with no discernable adverse effect on liver function. In reproductive toxicity studies in pregnant rats or rabbits, VT-1161 was not teratogenic at any exposure studied. In a recent CYP3A4 drug-drug interaction study utilizing midazolam as the substrate, no clinically significant inhibition and only weak induction of midazolam metabolism was observed. These data are favorable when compared to other oral antifungal agents such as fluconazole. This study is to evaluate the efficacy and safety of VT-1161 compared to fluconazole in treating acute VVC infections in RVVC subjects.
L1681: ## **Objectives:**
L1682: ## **Primary:**
L1683: - To evaluate the efficacy of oral VT-1161 in the prevention of culture-verified acute episodes of VVC through Week 50 in RVVC subjects.
- To compare the efficacy of oral VT-1161 and fluconazole in the treatment of an acute VVC episode in RVVC subjects.
L1684: ## **Secondary:**
L1685: - To evaluate the safety and tolerability of oral VT-1161 through Week 50.
L1686: ## **Methodology:**
L1687: ## **Study Design:**
L1688: This is a Phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of VT-1161 oral capsules versus fluconazole in the treatment of acute vulvovaginal candidiasis episodes in subjects with recurrent vulvovaginal candidiasis. In addition, the study will evaluate the efficacy of oral VT-1161 compared to placebo in the prevention of RVVC through Week 50 after treating the acute episodes of VVC with treatment of VT-1161 or fluconazole. Approximately 180 subjects will be randomized, 120 subjects in the VT-1161 arm and 60 subjects in the fluconazole and placebo arm.
L1689: Subjects must have a history of RVVC, defined as at least 3 episodes of acute VVC in the past 12 months, as described below:
L1690: - Subjects must have an acute VVC episode at the time of Screening, with a documented positive potassium hydroxide (KOH) performed at the investigative site
- Subjects must also have had 2 or more additional episodes of acute VVC in the past 12 months, with at least 1 of the episodes documented by a positive confirmatory laboratory test (i.e., culture, PCR, Affirm test, KOH test, a documented Papanicolaou (Pap) test, or other approved diagnostic tests)
L1691: Subjects must also have an acute VVC episode at Screening, defined as a total signs and symptoms score of \ge 3.
L1692: The study consists of two Phases:
L1693: 1) an Induction Phase for the treatment of the acute VVC episode in which subjects will be randomized to receive one of the following:
L1694: - 600 mg VT-1161 (4 x 150 mg capsules) on Day 1 and 450 mg VT-1161 (3 x 150 mg capsules) on Day 2, or
- 3 sequential 150 mg oral doses (every 72 hours) of over-encapsulated fluconazole.
L1695: 2) a Maintenance Phase in which subjects initially randomized to receive VT-1161 in the Induction Phase will receive 150 mg VT-1161 (1 x 150 mg capsule) weekly for 11 weeks and will then enter a 37-week follow-up period. Subjects randomized to receive over-encapsulated fluconazole in the Induction Phase will receive placebo weekly for 11 weeks and will then enter a 37-week follow-up period.
L1696: Once subjects have provided informed consent or assent (for those ages 12-17) the investigational site
L1697: will evaluate all subjects by completing a review of pertinent medical history, obtaining vital signs, weight and height, electrocardiogram (ECG) and laboratory tests, reviewing clinical signs and symptoms of vulvovaginitis, performing a complete physical examination including speculum examination of the vagina, performing a KOH wet mount test from a vaginal smear to confirm the presence of yeast, and collecting vaginal swabs to establish a baseline culture for identification of fungal species. Eligible subjects must have an acute VVC episode at Screening, defined as a total signs and symptoms score of \ge 3 and a positive local KOH wet mount preparation from a vaginal smear revealing filamentous hyphae/pseudohyphae and/or budding yeast cells, and must meet other initial entry criteria. Once eligibility is confirmed, subjects will be randomized to enter the Induction Phase.
L1698: At Screening (Day 1), subjects presenting with an acute infection and meeting all study eligibility criteria will enter the Induction Phase where they will be randomized 2:1 ratio to receive either 600 mg VT-1161 (4 x 150 mg capsules) on Day 1 and 450 mg VT-1161 (3 x 150 mg capsules) on Day 2, or 3 sequential 150 mg oral doses (every 72 hours) of over-encapsulated fluconazole. Subjects will return approximately 14 days after the first dose of VT-1161 or over-encapsulated fluconazole to determine if the acute VVC infection has resolved (defined by a signs and symptoms score of &lt;3). If the acute VVC has not resolved, the subject will be considered a screen failure and encouraged to see their physician for follow-up care.
L1699: All subjects in which the acute VVC infection has resolved will continue into a Maintenance Phase. Subjects initially randomized to receive VT-1161 in the Induction Phase will receive 150 mg VT-1161 (1 x 150 mg capsule) weekly for 11 weeks and will then enter a 37-week follow-up period. Whereas subjects randomized to receive over-encapsulated fluconazole in the Induction Phase will receive placebo weekly for 11 weeks and will then enter a 37-week follow-up period.
L1700: Subject's Day 1 (Screening) is defined as the first day of investigational medicinal product (IMP) administration and subsequent study days are defined by the number of consecutive days thereafter. Subject's Day 14 is defined as the first day of weekly dosing of IMP administration.
L1701: ## **Study Duration:**
L1702: The study duration is expected to be approximately 18 months from screening of the first subject until last follow-up of the last subject.
L1703: ## **Subject Participation:**
L1704: The duration of individual subject participation will be approximately 50 weeks. See Table 1 for the Schedule of Assessments and Procedures which will be performed. After Screening, subjects will return to the clinic on Day 14 (\pm 2 days), the start of the Maintenance Phase. Subjects will then have visits on Weeks 8 (\pm 7 days), 14 (\pm 14 days) (end of treatment [EOT]), 20 (\pm 14 days), 26 (\pm 14 days), 32 (\pm 14 days), 38 (\pm 14 days), 44 (\pm 14 days) and 50 (\pm 14 days) (end of study [EOS]). Subjects who return to the clinic for a recurrent acute VVC episode should be evaluated as per the unscheduled visit noted in the Schedule of Assessment and Procedures.
L1705: ## **Scoring of Vulvovaginal Signs and Symptoms:**
L1706: Vulvovaginal signs and symptoms will be evaluated at Screening and at each subsequent study visit. Each of the following vulvovaginal signs and symptoms will be scored and the individual scores combined, for a maximum score of 18 using the following scale:
L1707: - Signs: erythema, edema, and excoriation
- Symptoms: itching, burning, and irritation
L1708: Scoring Scale: Each score should be graded on a scale of 0 to 3 as follows:
L1709: - 0 = none (complete absence of any sign or symptom)
- 1 = mild (slight)
- 2 = moderate (present)
- 3 = severe (marked, intense)
L1710: ## **Mycology Assessment:**
L1711: A local mycological assessment using KOH wet mount followed by microscopy will be evaluated at the Screening (Day 1) and at any study visit where a recurrent acute VVC episode is suspected. In addition, a vaginal swab for culture testing will be obtained and sent to the central mycology laboratory at the Screening and each subsequent study visit.
L1712: ## **Plasma Pharmacokinetics:**
L1713: Blood samples for assay of VT-1161 plasma concentrations will be collected from subjects at Day 14, Week 14 and Week 50 visits.
L1714: Intense sampling will be obtained from approximately 12 consenting subjects at participating sites on Day 1 and on Day 2, at time points predose and 1, 2, 4, and 8 hours after dosing.
L1715: ## **Safety:**
L1716: Subject safety will be monitored by collection of adverse events (AEs), changes in vital signs, physical and vaginal exam parameters, ECGs, and safety laboratory parameters, including pregnancy tests for women of childbearing potential (WOCBP).
L1717: ## **Concomitant Medications:**
L1718: The following prescription and over-the-counter drug products are prohibited during the study and will be considered a protocol deviation:
L1719: - Topical or oral antifungal drugs (with the exception of fluconazole when prescribed by the Investigator to treat a subject with a recurrent VVC episode. If subject fails to respond to fluconazole treatment, the Investigator may employ treatment options corresponding to the site's standard of care treatment to treat the infection upon consult with the Medical Monitor.)
- Topical products applied to the vulva or vagina (e.g. antibiotic, antitrichomonal, corticosteroids, or anti-inflammatory agents).
- Oral antibacterial or antitrichomonal agents for the treatment of bacterial vaginosis, trichomoniasis, or other concomitant urogenital infection unless prescribed by the Investigator.
- Oral or injectable corticosteroid or immunosuppressive drugs for the duration of the study. Use of topical (no vulvar or vaginal steroids), inhaled, ophthalmic, or intraarticular and intralesional
L1720: steroids is permitted.
5. Drugs with a narrow therapeutic index that are metabolized by CYP3A4 and sensitive to induction of CYP3A4 (carbamazepine, cyclosporine, fentanyl, quinidine, sirolimus and tacrolimus).
Number of Subjects (Planned):
Approximately 180 randomized subjects are planned for this study. Assuming a 20% discontinuation rate for the duration of the study, approximately 144 subjects are expected to complete the study.
Criteria for Inclusion and Exclusion:
Criteria for Inclusion:Subjects must be generally healthy, post-menarchal females 12 years of age and older as of Screening (Day 1).
Subjects must have a history of recurrent VVC as defined by three (3) or more episodes of acute VVC in the past 12 months, including the episode confirmed at Screening, with at least one episode (not including the current episode) documented by a positive culture, PCR, Affirm test, KOH test, or a documented Pap test in the prior 12 months revealing filamentous hyphae/pseudohyphae and/or budding yeast cells.
Subjects must have an acute VVC episode at Screening, defined as a total signs and symptoms score of \ge 3 and a positive KOH wet mount preparation from a vaginal smear revealing filamentous hyphae/pseudohyphae and/or budding yeast cells.
Subjects must have a composite vulvovaginal signs and symptoms score of <3 at the Day 14 Visit.
Subjects must have a documented Pap test results at Screening or within the current standard of care guidelines for the appropriate age requirement, reported as either "negative for intraepithelial lesion or malignancy" or "ASCUS-atypical squamous cells of undetermined significance" (not applicable to subjects with a history of total hysterectomy).
Subjects must be suitable candidates for oral therapy and be able to swallow capsules intact.
Subjects must be willing and able to provide written informed consent or assent (for those ages 12-17) and authorization for use of protected health information.
Subjects must be willing and able to comply with protocol requirements, instructions, and protocol-stated restrictions and be likely to complete the study as planned.
Subjects of non-childbearing potential must meet the requirements defined below:Pre-menopausal with documentation of surgical sterilization (i.e., hysterectomy, bilateral tubal ligation, bilateral oophorectomy, or bilateral salpingectomy) at least 3 months prior to study entry.
Post-menopausal defined as amenorrhea for at least 12 months following cessation of all exogenous hormonal treatments at Screening.

Subjects of childbearing potential (includes ages 12-17) must use 1 of the following methods of contraception during the study through Week 50:
OPTION 1 - Highly effective methods that can be used alone:Copper intrauterine device used continuously and successfully for at least 90 days prior to first dose of study druga
Levonorgestrel-releasing intrauterine system used successfully for at least 90 days prior to first dose of study druga
Progestin implant used successfully for at least 90 days prior to first dose of study druga
L1721: - Monogamous with a vasectomized male partner (vasectomy performed at least 6 months prior to first dose of study drug)a
- Abstinenceb
L1722: ## OPTION 2 – Acceptable first and second barrier methods to be used in combination:
L1723: ## FIRST (*Hormonal Contraception*)a
L1724: - Estrogen &amp; progestin oral contraceptives, transdermal patch or vaginal ring used successfully for at least 90 days prior to first dose of study drug
- Progestin only oral contraceptives or injection used successfully for at least 90 days prior to first dose of study drug
L1725: ## SECOND (*Barrier Method*)
L1726: - Diaphragm (with spermicide)
- Cervical cap (with spermicide)
- Male condom (with or without spermicide)
L1727: In addition to OPTIONS 1 and 2, subjects of childbearing potential (includes ages 12-17) may also use 1 of the following methods of contraception after completing the Induction Phase on Study Day 14 through Week 50:
L1728: ## OPTION 3 (Day 14 – Week 50)c – Acceptable first and second barrier methods to be used in combination:
L1729: ## FIRST (*Barrier Method*)
L1730: - Diaphragm (with spermicide)
- Cervical cap (with spermicide)
L1731: ## SECOND (*Barrier Method*)
L1732: - Male condom (with or without spermicide)
L1733: a Subjects who started using an intrauterine device or any of the hormonal contraceptive methods described above less than 3 months prior to the first dose of study medication must agree to use a double-barrier method (i.e., diaphragm plus spermicide or condom) through 3 months after start of the use of the IUD or hormonal contraceptive. Similarly, subjects that claim a monogamous relationship with a vasectomized male partner where the vasectomy was performed less than 6 months prior to the first dose of study medication, must agree to use a double-barrier method (i.e., diaphragm plus spermicide or condom) through 6 months after the date of the vasectomy.
L1734: b Sexual abstinence is defined as refraining from heterosexual intercourse during the entire study period and where this is the usual life style of the subject. Periodic abstinence (calendar, symptothermal, postovulation methods), withdrawal (coitus interruptus), spermicides only, and lactational amenorrhoea method are not considered to be acceptable methods of contraception.
L1735: c Because of the teratogenic risk associated with repeat dosing of fluconazole, OPTION 3 contraceptive methods cannot be used during Screening and the Induction Phase of the study [from Screening (Day 1) until Day 14 of the study].
L1736: ## Criteria for Exclusion:
L1737: - Subjects must not have the presence of concomitant vulvovaginitis caused by other pathogens (e.g., bacterial vaginosis, *Trichomonas vaginalis*, *Chlamydia trachomatis*, or *Neisseria gonorrhoeae*) at Screening (Day 1) visit.
- Subjects must not have an active HPV infection as evidenced by visible condylomas on vulvovaginal examination at Screening (Day 1) visit.
- Subjects must not have the presence or a history of another vaginal or vulvar condition(s) that in
L1738: the Investigator's opinion would confound the interpretation of clinical response.

- Subjects must not have a history of cervical cancer.
- Subjects must not use any systemic (e.g., oral or injectable) corticosteroid therapy during the study or within 30 days prior to Screening visit; nor use of topical vulvar or vaginal steroids during the study or within 7 days prior to Screening visit. However, use of topical (no vulvar or vaginal steroids), inhaled, ophthalmic, collunarium/nasal, intraarticular and intralesional steroids is permitted.
- Subjects must not use any systemic (e.g., oral or injectable) or topical (applied to the vulva or vaginal area) antifungal, antibacterial, or antitrichomonal drugs for the treatment of bacterial vaginosis, trichomoniasis, or other concomitant urogenital infection during the study (unless prescribed by the Investigator) or within 7 days prior to Screening.
- Subjects must not be using drugs with a narrow therapeutic index that are metabolized by CYP3A4 and sensitive to induction of CYP3A4 during the study (carbamazepine, cyclosporine, fentanyl, quinidine, sirolimus and tacrolimus).
- Subjects must not use any vaginal estrogen replacement therapy, ospemifene, Vitamin E gel capsules (vaginally) or lubricants within 7 days prior to Screening.
- Subjects must not have a condition that would require treatment during the study with concomitant topical (applied to vulva and vagina) or systemic antimicrobial therapy for any reason.
- Subjects must not have received an immunosuppressive medication (e.g. cyclosporine, tacrolimus, methotrexate, 6-mercaptopurine, mycophenolate, etc.), or radiation therapy within 3 months prior to Screening or have a medical condition where it would be likely that the subject may need to use these therapies during the study.
- Subjects must not have evidence of any clinically significant major organ disease or current clinically significant infection or any other conditions (with the exception of acute VVC) that may affect the clinical assessment of RVVC per investigator judgment.
- Subjects must not have any comorbid condition that in the opinion of the Investigator would preclude the safe participation of the subject in the study or would prevent the subject from meeting the study requirements.
- Subjects must not have any condition that in the opinion of the investigator could impact drug absorption (e.g. gastrectomy, Roux-en-Y gastric bypass surgery, gastric bands or staples, etc.), distribution, or elimination.
- Subjects must not have poorly controlled diabetes mellitus (HbA1c \ge 8.5\% at Screening; performed only on known diabetics or subjects suspected to have diabetes by the investigative site).
- Subjects must not have moderate or severe hepatic and/or renal disease (defined in Appendix B and Appendix C).
- Subjects must not have a laboratory abnormality that in the opinion of the Investigator is likely to introduce additional risk to the subject or might interfere with data interpretation. The specific findings listed below are excluded at Day 1 (a single repeat laboratory evaluation is allowed for eligibility determination, except for HIV, HBsAg and antibodies to hepatitis C virus):Serum alanine aminotransferase (ALT) \ge 2.0 \times the upper limit of normal (ULN) of the reference range.
- Serum aspartate aminotransferase (AST) \ge 2.0 \times the ULN of the reference range.
- Serum total bilirubin \ge 1.5 \times the ULN of the reference range, unless the elevation is consistent with Gilbert's Syndrome.
- Electrocardiographic QTc interval &gt; 470 msec as corrected by the Fridericia formula, or any clinically significant electrocardiographic abnormality.
L1739: e. Positive test for antibodies to human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg), or antibodies to hepatitis C virus.

- Subjects of childbearing potential must not be pregnant (positive pregnancy test at Screening), lactating, or planning to become pregnant during the study period.
- Subjects must not have planned surgery or other medical procedures that in the Investigator's opinion would impact compliance with the protocol.
- Subjects must not have active substance abuse (e.g., drugs of any kind, including alcohol) or any other medical, psychiatric or social condition that in the Investigator's opinion would preclude compliance with the protocol.
- Subjects must not have received VT-1161 study medication in a previous study.
- Subjects must not have received any IMP in a clinical trial within 5 half-lives of that IMP prior to Screening (if unknown, 60 days prior to Screening).
- Subjects must not have a known history of intolerance or hypersensitivity to azole antifungal drugs (e.g., fluconazole, itraconazole, voriconazole, posaconazole, and isavuconazole), or any excipients of the capsule formulation.
L1740: ## **Induction Treatment Phase**
L1741: **Investigational Medicinal Product:** VT-1161 150 mg capsules
L1742: ## **Dose and Mode of Administration:**
L1743: Oral administration of 600 mg VT-1161 (4 x 150 mg capsules) on Day 1, and 450 mg VT-1161 (3 x 150 mg capsules) on Day 2.
L1744: Oral administration of IMP within 30 minutes after ingestion of the subject's main meal of the day (as determined by the subject and kept consistent throughout the duration of the study). Approximately 240 mL (approx. 8oz) of water is to be consumed with each dose of IMP.
L1745: Placebo capsules matching the VT-1161 capsules will be administered on Day 1 (4 capsules) and Day 2 (3 capsules) to maintain study blind for those subjects randomized to the fluconazole treatment arm.
L1746: **Investigational Medicinal Product:** Fluconazole 150 mg tablets, over-encapsulated
L1747: ## **Dose and Mode of Administration:**
L1748: Oral administration of 3 sequential doses (every 72 hours) of fluconazole 150 mg starting on Day 1.
L1749: Oral administration of IMP within 30 minutes after ingestion of the subject's main meal of the day (as determined by the subject and kept consistent throughout the duration of the study). Approximately 240 mL (approx. 8oz) of water is to be consumed with each dose of IMP.
L1750: Placebo capsules matching the over-encapsulated fluconazole IMP will be administered on Day 1, 4 and 7 to maintain study blind for those subjects randomized to the VT-1161 treatment arm.
L1751: ## **Maintenance Treatment Phase**
L1752: **Maintenance Phase Test Product:** VT-1161 150 mg capsules
L1753: ## **Dose and Mode of Administration:**
L1754: Oral administration of VT-1161 within 30 minutes after ingestion of the subject's main meal of the day (as determined by the subject and kept consistent throughout the duration of the study). Approximately 240 mL (approx. 8oz) of water is to be consumed with each dose of IMP.
L1755: Starting on Day 14, VT-1161 150 mg (1 capsule) will be given once weekly for 11 weeks.
Duration of Administration: 11 weeks
Maintenance Phase Reference Product: Matching Placebo capsules
Dose and Mode of Administration:
Oral administration of placebo within 30 minutes after ingestion of the subject's main meal of the day (as determined by the subject and kept consistent throughout the duration of the study). Approximately 240 mL (approx. 8oz) of water is to be consumed with each dose.
Starting on Day 14, placebo (1 capsule) will be given once weekly for 11 weeks.
Duration of Administration: 11 weeks
Study Populations:
Intent-to-Treat (ITT) Population: All randomized subjects.
Safety Population: All randomized subjects who received at least 1 dose of study drug.
Per Protocol Population (PP): All randomized subjects who had the following:
Had no deviations to inclusion/exclusion criteria that could impact treatment outcome.
Were compliant with the assigned study treatment, defined as \ge 80\% compliance during the Induction Phase and \ge 50\% compliance during the Maintenance Phase.
Had the Week 50 visit completed within the acceptable time window (\pm 14 days).
Had no major protocol violations that would impact treatment outcome.

Modified ITT: All enrolled subjects who had the following:
Positive KOH at Screening.
Positive culture at Screening.
Negative culture at Day 14.

Criteria for Evaluation:
Efficacy:
Clinical and mycological assessments will be conducted as outlined in Table 1. Schedule of Assessments and Procedures.
All efficacy analyses will be performed on the ITT, PP, and mITT Populations.
The primary efficacy outcome measure is the proportion of subjects with one or more culture-verified acute VVC episodes (post-randomization through Week 50) in the ITT population, which will include the subjects who failed clearing their infection during the Induction Phase. An acute VVC episode (considered a recurrent episode) is defined as a positive culture for Candida species and a clinical signs and symptoms score of \ge 3.
The key secondary efficacy outcome measures include the following:
L1756: - The proportion of subjects with resolved acute VVC infections (clinical signs and symptoms score of &lt;3) at Day 14 following treatment with VT-1161 or fluconazole.
- The proportion of subjects with at least one culture-verified acute VVC episode with signs and symptoms of \ge 3 during the Maintenance Phase (post Day 14 through Week 50).
- Time to first recurrence of a culture-verified acute VVC episode with signs and symptoms score \ge 3 during the Maintenance Phase (post Day 14 through Week 50).
- The proportion of subjects with at least one positive culture for *Candida* during the Maintenance Phase.
L1757: ## **Safety:**
L1758: All safety analyses will be performed on the Safety Population. AEs will be collected throughout the study. Physical and vaginal examination findings and vital signs will be recorded at Screening and throughout the study per Table 1.
L1759: ## **Schedule of Assessments and Procedures.**
L1760: ECGs and safety laboratory tests (hematology, chemistry, and urinalysis) will be obtained throughout the study. All concomitant medications taken during the period from 30 days prior to Screening through the Week 50 (EOS) visit will be recorded in the case report form (CRF). Pertinent and all major medical conditions will be recorded on the Medical History CRF.
L1761: Subjects who want to discontinue from the study should be encouraged to remain in the study for all remaining visits through Week 50 for safety and efficacy assessments. All subjects who discontinue early from the study should have all EOS procedures performed on the day of discontinuation.
L1762: ## **Statistical Methods and Data Analysis:**
L1763: A statistical analysis plan will be finalized before 50% of subjects are enrolled.
L1764: ## **Efficacy Analysis:**
L1765: The statistical objectives of this study will be:
L1766: - To determine if there is a difference in the proportions of subjects with one or more episodes of culture-verified acute VVC in those receiving VT-1161 compared to those receiving placebo
- To determine if there is a difference in the ability of VT-1161 and fluconazole to resolve acute episodes in subjects with RVVC.
L1767: A sample size of 82 subjects in the VT-1161 arm and 41 subjects in the fluconazole/placebo arm provides at least 90% power to detect a treatment difference between the VT-1161 treatment group and the fluconazole/placebo treatment group with a type 1 error rate of 0.05. Sample size calculation assumes 50% of the subjects in the placebo arm will have at least one culture-verified acute VVC episode post-randomization through Week 50. For the first key secondary endpoint, the proportion of subjects with resolved acute VVC infections (clinical signs and symptoms score of &lt;3) at Day 14 following treatment with VT-1161 or fluconazole, a sample size of 120 subjects in the VT-1161 arm and 60 subjects in the fluconazole/placebo arm provides at least 88% power to detect non-inferiority between the VT-1161 treatment group and the fluconazole treatment group with a non-inferiority margin of 15% and a type 1 error rate of 0.05. The sample size calculation assumes that 90% of the subjects in the fluconazole arm will resolve their acute VVC infection by Day 14.
L1768: For the efficacy analyses, missing values for the investigator's assessment of clinical signs and symptoms or the culture result will be imputed using the method of multiple imputation. For subjects who discontinue the study early and have missing assessments for all visits after discontinuation, the missing
L1769: values for the expected scheduled visits will be imputed using the method of multiple imputation. The missing values will be imputed using the following auxiliary information: region, treatment, baseline BMI, baseline age, ethnicity, and visit.
L1770: A chi-square test will be used to compare the proportion of subjects with one or more culture-verified acute VVC in those receiving VT-1161 compared to those receiving fluconazole and placebo. The proportion of subjects who have cleared their infection at Day 14 in those receiving VT-1161 will be evaluated for non-inferiority versus those receiving fluconazole utilizing a non-inferiority margin of 15%.
L1771: ## Safety Analysis:
L1772: AEs will be coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities® dictionary. The number and percentage of subjects reporting treatment-emergent AEs will be tabulated by system organ class, preferred term, and greatest severity with a breakdown by treatment group. Mean changes from pre-treatment in vital signs, ECG, and clinical laboratory variables will be summarized by treatment group.
L1773: ## Pharmacokinetics:
L1774: No formal statistical hypothesis testing related to pharmacokinetics will be conducted. VT-1161 concentrations will be presented by visit with descriptive statistics. The pharmacokinetic data will be used in a cross-population pharmacokinetic analysis that will be presented in a separate report.
L1775: Blood samples for assay of VT-1161 plasma concentrations will be collected from subjects prior to IMP on Day 14 and Week 14 and at Week 50. Intense sampling will be obtained only from consenting subjects at participating sites on Day 1 and Day 2 (predose and 1, 2, 4, and 8 hours after dosing, with ±10-minute window per timepoint).

In the Induction Phase, IMP may be over-encapsulated and matching placebo capsules will be provided to maintain study blind. Subjects participating in the intense PK collection will be required to take the Days 1 and 2 IMP at the Investigational site.

L1781: ## 16. Efficacy: Additional Information and Assessment
L1782: ## 16.1. Additional Analyses for Study CL-011
L1783: ## Screening Characteristics for Study CL-011
L1784: Table 150 summarizes key screening signs and symptoms and microbiologic characteristics of the ITT population. Note that randomization had not occurred at the time of screening. This analysis looks back at the screening period to determine what characteristics these subjects had. Additionally, this information does not include the subjects who entered into the screening phase but were not randomized.
L1785: At screening the placebo arm had a numerically higher signs and symptoms score compared to the oteseconazole arm. *Candida albicans* accounted for the majority of screening pathogens in both arms (51.6% in the oteseconazole and 49.5% in the placebo), followed by *C. glabrata* (4.6% and 7.3%, respectively). There were no mixed *Candida* pathogens. A sizeable percentage of subjects in each arm had negative *Candida* cultures at screening, 38% and 35%, respectively.

L1817: ## 16.2. Additional Analyses for Study CL-012
L1818: ## Screening Characteristics for Study CL-012
L1819: **Table 154** summarizes key screening signs and symptoms and microbiologic characteristics of the ITT population. Note that randomization had not occurred at the time of screening. This analysis looks back at the screening period to determine what characteristics these subjects had. Additionally, this information does not include the subjects who entered into the screening phase but were not randomized.
L1820: At screening the placebo arm had a numerically higher signs and symptoms score compared to the oteseconazole arm. *Candida albicans* accounted for the majority of screening pathogens in both arms (46.3% in the oteseconazole and 56.5% in the placebo), followed by *C. glabrata* (4.1% and 6.5%, respectively). There were a few patients with mixed *Candida* pathogens. A sizeable percentage of subjects in each arm had negative *Candida* cultures at screening, 39.4% and 31.5%, respectively.

L1853: ## 16.3. Additional Analyses for Study CL-017
L1854: ## Applicant's Original Analysis of Primary Endpoint
L1855: As stated in the protocol, the primary efficacy outcome measure was the proportion of subjects with 1 or more culture-verified acute VVC episodes (post-randomization through Week 50) in the ITT Population, which included the subjects who failed clearing their infection during the Induction Phase. Rather than considering all subjects who failed clearing their infection during the Induction Phase as having an event, as intended, the Applicant interpreted this as including such subject in the ITT population but only considered them an event if they met the definition of culture-verified acute VVC at Day 14. Only 2 of the 10 oteseconazole Induction failures and 2 of the 3 fluconazole Induction failures had a culture confirmed acute VVC episode at Day 14 and were considered as having a recurrence in the Applicant's original analysis. Since Induction Failures did not continue to the Maintenance Phase, the remaining subjects by default had missing data for the remainder of the study. Therefore, Applicant's misinterpretation of the intended primary endpoint impacted the analysis using MI for handling missing data but not the analysis where missing data were considered failures. **Table 158** presents the results using MI for handling missing data as reported in the CL-017 Clinical Study Report.
L1856: **Table 158. Applicant's Original Analysis of Proportion of Subjects Who Had ≥1 Culture-Verified Acute VVC Episodes During Post- Randomization Through Week 50 for Trial CL-017: ITT Population**

L1907: ## 16.4. Additional Analyses for Studies CL-011, CL-012 and CL-017
L1908: ## Timing of Recurrence – During Maintenance Phase or Off-Treatment Follow-Up
L1909: The three efficacy studies included a treatment period of the initial episode of VVC, a maintenance phase consisting of 12 weeks of treatment, and an extended follow-up period. Determining when recurrences occur is important to understand if oteseconazole reduces recurrences only during the maintenance period or if the effect of oteseconazole continues during the off-treatment follow-up phase.
L1910: The time to recurrence during the study periods are summarized in the following tables for the three trials. Note that the largest number of recurrences on placebo occurred during the first 12 weeks of follow-up (while oteseconazole subjects were receiving the maintenance regimen). The percentages of recurrences on the oteseconazole arms were similar across the different 12-week time frames.

L1923: ## **Accidental Unblinding in Studies CL-011 and CL-012**
L1924: Due to an error in the Interactive Response Technology system, automated emails disclosing unblinded randomization details were initially sent to the site user performing randomization in studies CL-011 and CL-012. The error in the Interactive Response Technology system was active from the start of these two studies until corrected on November 28, 2018 after which site personnel randomizing subjects no longer received the unblinded email. Unblinded emails were sent to 17 different users from 16 sites and involved the first 24 subjects of studies CL-011 (14
L1925: subjects) and CL-012 (10 subjects). This issue did not impact CL-017, which was initiated in 2019.
L1926: The Applicant was notified of this error by the contractor, (b)(4) on November 29, 2018. On the same day, the Applicant requested that the Clinical Lead (b)(4) or assigned clinical research associate contact the affected sites to investigate a) site awareness of the unblinded communication, b) if any potential dissemination of the information to other site staff or clinical research associates had occurred, c) and what actions were taken by site staff who received the unblinding email. Additionally, the Applicant sent a Note To File to the Principal Investigators on January 16, 2019 regarding the unblinding and requesting each sign a statement confirming adherence to good clinical practices (GCPs) and retrain staff on GCP particularly unblinding procedures.
L1927: The impacted sites and subjects were the following:
L1928: - Study CL-011:Site 102- Subjects (b)(6)
- Site 103- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 105- Subjects (b)(6)
- Site 107- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 108- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 114- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 132- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 136- Subjects (b)(6)
- Site 147- Subjects (b)(6)
- Study CL-012:Site 204- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 215- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 218- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 229- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 233- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 240- Subjects (b)(6)
- Site 244- Subject (b)(6)
- Site 245- Subject (b)(6)
L1929: The Applicant removed all 24 subjects where accidental unblinding emails were sent to the site from the per-protocol population. However, they included 21 of the 24 subjects in the ITT population since none of the subjects were notified of treatment assignment and the study coordinators who received the unblinded email did not perform vaginal exams nor evaluate VVC signs and symptoms (with possibly the exception of site 136). The 3 subjects removed were from site 240 which had other GCP noncompliance issues and was removed from all analyses.
L1930: Since the ITT population is the primary analysis population, sensitivity analyses on the primary endpoint excluding the subjects with accidental unblinding as well as all subjects at the sites where unblinded emails were received were conducted to determine the robustness of the overall results based on the missing=recurrence. In CL-011, accidental unblinding occurred for 9 oteseconazole subjects (3 had recurrence, 2 had incomplete follow-up but no known recurrence, and 4 did not have a recurrence) and 4 placebo subjects (4 had recurrence and 1 did not have a
L1931: recurrence). In Study CL-012, 4 oteseconazole subjects (1 had recurrence and 3 did not have a recurrence) and 3 placebo subjects (1 each had recurrence, incomplete follow-up but no known recurrence, and no recurrence) in the ITT population had accidental unblinding. The results of these sensitivity analysis are summarized in Table 166 and are robust to excluding the subjects/sites with accidental unblinding.

L1937: ## 16.5. Trial CL-006 Trial Design
L1938: Trial CL-006 was a Phase 2 multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group, dose ranging study evaluating the safety and efficacy of oteseconazole at two different doses (150 mg and 300 mg) and at two different dosing durations (12 weeks and 24 weeks). Females were recruited from 38 clinical sites within the United States.
L1939: The trial consisted of a 1-week open-label Induction Phase where eligible, consenting participants aged \ge 18 to &lt;65 years old with: (a) an acute VVC infection, (b) a history of at least 3 or more acute episodes of symptomatic VVC within a 12-month period, and (c) a composite vulvovaginal signs and symptoms score of \ge 3 received treatment with three sequential doses of fluconazole 150 mg administered every 72 hours. Fourteen days after the initial dose of fluconazole was administered, all females with a composite vulvovaginal signs and symptoms score of &lt; 3 were randomized in a 1:1:1:1:1 fashion into the Maintenance Phase and were assigned to one of five treatment groups:
L1940: - VT-1161 low-dose (150 mg) for 12 weeks, then placebo for 12 weeks
- VT-1161 low-dose (150 mg) for 24 weeks
- VT-1161 high-dose (300 mg) for 12 weeks, then placebo for 12 weeks
- VT-1161 high-dose (300 mg) for 24 weeks
- Placebo (administered to match the VT-1161 treatment groups)
L1941: During the Maintenance Phase, participants, who were blinded to treatment as were the study investigator and the study and clinical staff, received low dose oteseconazole (150 mg + placebo) or high-dose oteseconazole daily (300 mg) for Days 1 (Baseline) through 7. Afterwards, participants received once weekly oteseconazole for either Weeks 2-12, if the participant was randomly assigned to 12-weeks of treatment or from Weeks 2-24, if the participant was randomly assigned to 24-weeks (6-month) of treatment.
L1942: Females eligible for study participation were generally healthy, non-pregnant, nonlactating females between the ages of \ge 18 and &lt;65 years old who were diagnosed with symptomatic acute VVC; had a history of RVVC—3 or more symptomatic episodes of acute VVC in the past 12 months, including the episode at Screening; with a signs and symptoms score of \ge 3 at Screening
L1943: and a score of &lt;3 at the baseline visit, and a positive potassium hydroxide wet mount. Participants were excluded if they had concomitant vulvovaginitis from other infectious pathogens (e.g., bacterial vaginosis, *Trichomonas vaginalis*, *Chlamydia trachomatis*, or *Neisseria gonorrhoeae*). Females of childbearing potential were required to use one of several acceptable birth control methods throughout the study. Full eligibility criteria can be found in the Appendix.
L1944: Study visits included: a Screening visit, randomization on Day 1 (Baseline), with follow-up clinic visits on Day 7 (\pm 2 days), Week 4 (\pm 4 days), Week 8 (\pm 4 days), Week 12 (\pm 7 days), Week 18 (\pm 7 days), Week 24 (\pm 14 days), Week 36 (\pm 14 days), and Week 48 (\pm 14 days) in which efficacy, safety, laboratory, and PK assessments were conducted. Each subject was anticipated to remain in the study for approximately 50-weeks. The primary efficacy endpoint was the proportion of subjects with 1 or more culture-verified acute VVC episodes during the Maintenance Phase through Week 48 of the study and was analyzed in the ITT population -- the primary analysis population.
L1945: Key secondary efficacy endpoints included the:
L1946: - Proportion of subjects with culture-verified acute VVC infection through Week 24 of the study
- Proportion of subjects with culture-verified acute VVC infection by pathogen
- Time to first recurrence of culture-verified acute VVC infection;
L1947: The safety and tolerability of the four dosing regimens were evaluated as key secondary objectives. Safety analyses included the assessment of AEs, clinical laboratory evaluations, vital signs and physical examinations, and 12-lead ECGs.
L1948: A total of 254 subjects enrolled in the Induction Phase of CL-006 and 215 subjects were randomly assigned to the Maintenance Phase.
L1949: ## CL-006 Baseline Demographic and Clinical and Microbiologic Characteristics, ITT Population
L1950: Trial CL-006 was conducted solely in the United States. A total of 215 subjects comprised the ITT population with near equal numbers of subjects randomized to each treatment arm. Key baseline demographic characteristics in the ITT population are summarized in Table 167. No significant imbalances were observed across treatment groups. Mean (SD) and median age across all treatment arms were 34.6 (9.95) and 33.0, respectively. Conversely, the majority of subjects were in the 25-34 age cohort (37.2%) followed by the 35-44 age cohort (25.1%). There were no subjects in the &lt;18 year old cohort or in the \ge 65 year old cohorts. Over 50% of subjects were White and non-Latino, followed by Black/African-American, non-Latino subjects as seen across all treatment arms. Altogether, Latino subjects accounted for nearly 20% (18.6%) of the total ITT population. Subjects' mean (SD) and median BMIs were 27.3 and 25.6
L1951: Most subjects had 3 acute VVC episodes in the preceding 12 months (43.3%) prior to enrollment, closely followed by 4-6 acute VVC episodes in the preceding 12 months (39.5%). However, subjects in the oteseconazole 150-mg x 12 weeks arm had a higher percentage of subjects with 4-6 and 7-9 acute VVC episodes in the preceding 12 months when compared to their counterparts in the 3 other arms. Mean (SD) and median numbers of acute VVC episodes in the preceding 12 months for the entire ITT population were 4.5 (2.6) and 4.0, respectively.

L1973: ## CL-006 Efficacy Results
L1974: ## Primary Efficacy Endpoint
L1975: The proportion of subjects with 1 or more culture-verified acute VVC infections through Week 48 for the ITT population was lower in the VT-1161 treatment groups (2 [4.8%] subjects in the 150 mg 12 weeks group, 3 [7.0%] subjects in the 150 mg 24 weeks group, 0 [0.0%] subjects in the 300 mg 12 weeks group, and 2 [4.9%] subjects in the 300 mg 24 weeks group) compared with the placebo group (24 [52.2%] subjects). The differences were statistically significant in each of the VT-1161 treatment groups compared with placebo (p&lt;0.0001) (Table 168). The results observed in the mITT, mITT2, mITT3, and PP populations were similar to those recorded in the ITT population.
L1976: The results of sensitivity analyses, subgroup analyses, and exploratory analyses were similar to the primary analysis findings.

L2014: ## 17.1. Nonfatal Treatment-Emergent SAEs Narratives
L2015: ## 17.1.1. Trial CL-011 Nonfatal Treatment-Emergent SAEs
L2016: ## CL-011 Oteseconazole Arm
L2017: ## Subject (b)(6) (Oteseconazole) (PTs: Tubo-Ovarian Abscess and Endometriosis)
L2018: A 45-year-old White, non-Latino Canadian female with a past medical history noted for asthma, fibromyalgia, pelvic pain, and six prior episodes of VVC in the past 12 months. The subject was randomized into the Maintenance Phase of the study and received the first dose of oteseconazole on Study Day 1. The subject received the last dose of oteseconazole on Study Day 84.
L2019: On Study Day 31, the subject presented to the emergency room with a complaint of persistent pelvic pain. On Study Day 38, the subject was diagnosed with a left tubo-ovarian abscess, via ultrasound, and was then hospitalized.
L2020: Key concomitant medications included: piperacillin-tazobactam (Study Days 38-40) and was later switched to oral moxifloxacin (Study Days 40-52).
L2021: On Study Day 60, the subject underwent a bilateral ovariectomy/hysterectomy for removal of the left tubo-ovarian abscess, when an endometrioma (Study Day 60) was incidentally discovered. These two SAEs (tubo-ovarian abscess; Study Days 38-60) and (endometriosis; Study Day 60) were resolved on Study Day 60. The final dose of oteseconazole was given on Study Day 84.
L2022: *Study Investigator/Applicant Impression:* The Study Investigator determined that neither serious adverse event (SAE) was related to either fluconazole induction or oteseconazole maintenance.
L2023: *Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:* Clinical reviewer has reviewed accompanying data and narrative and agrees that the observed SAEs were unrelated to either study treatment.
L2024: ## Subject (b)(6) (Oteseconazole) (PT: Limb Traumatic Amputation)
L2025: A 51-year-old Black/African-American, non-Latino female with a past medical history most notable for anxiety/depression, iron deficiency anemia, menorrhagia, fibroids, and s/p tubal ligation (2001). The subject had three prior episodes of acute VVC in the past 12 months. The subject was randomized into the study's Maintenance Phase and received the first dose of oteseconazole on Study Day 1 and last dose on Study Day 87.
L2026: On Study Day 334, the subject reportedly sustained a treatment-emergent SAE coded to limb traumatic amputation (Study Days 334-358) secondary to a motor vehicle accident. Following this accident, they were sent emergently to the OR for debridement. The following day the subject underwent an open reduction and internal fixation for repair of their right radial fracture and anterior tibialis tendon. The subject required several operations and afterwards was transferred to a rehab facility. This event met three SAE criteria including: being life-threatening, resulting in persistent/significant disability and requiring hospitalization due to multiple fractures, including facial fractures, and abrasions.
L2027: NDA 215888
Vivjoa (oteseconazole)
L2028: ***Study Investigator/Applicant Impression:*** The Study Investigator determined that the reported events traumatic amputation of leg below left knee, facial fractures and motor vehicle accident were unrelated to fluconazole or oteseconazole. The outcome of the event was categorized as ongoing.
L2029: ***Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:*** Clinical reviewer has reviewed accompanying data and narratives and agrees that the observed SAE was unrelated of oteseconazole exposure.
L2030: ## **Subject** (b)(6) **(Oteseconazole) (PT: Postoperative Wound Infection)**
L2031: A 47-year-old Black/African American, non-Latino female with a prior medical history most notable for diabetes, hypertension, osteoarthritis, gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD), and five episodes of acute VVC in the past 12 months. The subject was randomized into the study's Maintenance Phase and received their first dose of oteseconazole on Study Day 1. They received their last dose on Study Day 25 and completed participation in the Maintenance Phase on Study Day 344.
L2032: The subject underwent a right knee replacement on Study Day 264. The post-operative course was complicated by pain, swelling, redness, and drainage at the incision site for which the subject presented at the hospital on Study Day 270 and was diagnosed with an infection (postoperative wound infection; Study Days 270-273). This occurrence was reported as a treatment-emergent SAE. The subject was discharged from hospital on Study Day 273. The outcome of the event was reported as resolved. Action taken was not applicable.
L2033: ***Study Investigator/Applicant Impression:*** The Study Investigator and Applicant assessed the event as being unrelated to fluconazole induction therapy or oteseconazole due to the lack of a temporal association between the event and receipt of the last dose of the oteseconazole (which was 8 months prior). Osteoarthritis and diabetes were predisposing factors.
L2034: ***Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:*** Clinical reviewer has reviewed accompanying data and narrative and agrees that the observed SAE was unrelated to oteseconazole exposure.
L2035: ## **Trial CL-011 Placebo Arm**
L2036: ## **Subject** (b)(6) **(Placebo) (PT: Appendicitis)**
L2037: A 25-year-old White, non-Latino Polish female with a past medical history significant for recurrent VVC, with a history of three acute VVC episodes in the prior 12 months. The subject was randomized into the study's Maintenance Phase and received the first dose of placebo on Study Day 1.
L2038: On Study Day 75, the subject developed lower abdominal pain, presented to the hospital and underwent a laparoscopic appendectomy for the treatment-emergent SAE (appendicitis; Study Days 75-78). The SAE ended on Study Day 78. The outcome of the event was reported as resolved.
L2039: ***Study Investigator/Applicant Impression:*** The Study Investigator assessed the event as being unrelated to receipt of placebo.
L2040: ***Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:*** Clinical reviewer has reviewed all accompanying data and the case narrative and agrees that the observed SAE was not due to exposure to placebo.
L2041: ## **Subject** (b)(6) **(Placebo) (PT: Vaginal Hemorrhage)**

L2151: ***Study Investigator's/Applicant's Impression:*** The Study Investigator and Applicant assessed SAE as an aspiration pneumonia secondary due to an accidental overdose, since the subject had ingested sertraline (Zoloft), prazosin (for treatment of high blood pressure), and alprazolam (Xanax) all superimposed upon recreational drug use. They believed the subject's underlying psychiatric history was a contributor to the reported SAE.
L2152: ***Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:*** Clinical reviewer has reviewed accompanying data and narrative and agrees with the Study Investigator and Applicant that the observed SAE was unrelated to receipt of placebo but likely the result of a participant with a polysubstance abuse and underlying mental health issues.
L2153: ## 17.1.4. Trial CL-006 Nonfatal Treatment-Emergent SAEs
L2154: ## Oteseconazole Arm
L2155: ## Subject (b)(6) (Oteseconazole 300-mg x24 Weeks) (PT: Meningitis, Viral)
L2156: A 20-year-old White, non-Latino female with a past medical history most notable for supraventricular tachycardia/Wolff-Parkinson-White syndrome (diagnosed (b)(6)), s/p heart ablation (diagnosed (b)(6)); left breast solitary mass (diagnosed September (b)(6)); and a history of RVVC (since (b)(6)). Participant was randomized into and received the first dose of oteseconazole 300-mg x 24 weeks on Study Day 1. The subject received the last dose of oteseconazole on Study Day 168. The subject completed the study on Study Day 329.
L2157: On Study Day 187, the subject was admitted to the hospital with fever, nausea, vomiting, epigastric pain, and intractable headaches for two days. A work-up including an abdominal ultrasound, a noncontrast CT of the head and brain, CXR, and a magnetic resonance angiogram (MRA) was unremarkable. Following a lumbar puncture, the subject was diagnosed with viral meningitis. Cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) cultures were negative.
L2158: NDA 215888
Vivjoa (oteseconazole)

L2161: The event was considered resolved on Study Day 191 and the subject was discharged from the hospital that same day with a discharge diagnosis of viral meningitis (Study Day 187-191).

L2164: ## **Subject (b)(6) (Oteseconazole 300-mg x24 Weeks) (PT: Bronchopneumonia
## [Hypotension])**
L2165: An 18-year-old White, non-Latino female with a past medical history most notable for anxiety, treated with benzodiazepine diazepam, recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs) (since (b)(6)), s/p motor vehicle accident (b)(6) with chronic back pain on chronic opiates, and a history of recurrent VVC (since (b)(6)). Subject was randomized into and received the first dose of oteseconazole 300-mg x 24 weeks on Study Day 1. The subject received the last dose of oteseconazole on Study Day 162. The subject completed the study on Study Day 305, however, the subject failed to complete the entire study due to being lost to follow-up.
L2166: On Study Day 216, the subject presented to a hospital emergency room with a complaint of bilateral flank pain, fever, dysuria, nausea, vomiting, dizziness, and fatigue. A CXR showed bilateral patchy infiltrates with bilateral bronchopneumonia (Study Days 216-218). A CT of the abdomen and pelvis showed left &gt; right basilar consolidation. Participant had persistent orthostatic hypotension for which the subject was administered a total of 4 liters of 0.9% normal saline. In the ED, the subject received ibuprofen, ondansetron, azithromycin, metronidazole, and ceftriaxone sodium. The following day, the subject was admitted under observation to the ICU where the subject was observed for persistent dizziness over 12 hours. Staff was initially concerned for pneumonia or aspiration given CXR findings and shortness of breath. Hypotension resolved on Study Day 218. Prior to being discharged from the hospital, the subject was counseled on narcotics use and the risk of sedation and constipation. Discharge diagnoses included constipation, chronic pain, anxiety, depression, and hematuria. During the study, this subject was additionally recorded as having the following nonserious TEAEs: constipation, arthralgia, back pain, musculoskeletal pain, nausea, flank pain, anemia, dysuria, and bronchopneumonia.
L2167: NDA 215888
Vivjoa (oteseconazole)
L2168: **Study Investigator's/Applicant's Impression:** The Study Investigator assessed the event as being unrelated to oteseconazole administration, given the absence of a temporal or causal relationship between the event and oteseconazole administration. In addition, there was an alternate biologically plausible alternate explanation for the observed event.
L2169: **Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:** Clinical reviewer has reviewed the subject's accompanying data and narratives and agrees that the observed SAE was unlikely the result of oteseconazole exposure. It appears that the subject's refractory hypotension was related to opiate abuse and further exacerbated by superimposed benzodiazepine abuse. Certain opiates can cause orthostatic hypotension which may result in dizziness. Opiates are also associated with constipation. Diazepam can cause hypotension as well. If the subject experienced alteration in mental status in the setting of opiate misuse, subject could very well have aspirated. The reviewer thinks that the described SAE would have more appropriately been coded as an accidental overdose.

L2175: ## **Subject (b)(6) (Oteseconazole 150-mg x12 Weeks) (PT: Abdominal Pain/Pancreatitis)**
L2176: A 36-year-old White, Latino female with a past medical history most significant for depression (since (b)(6)), anxiety, chronic pancreatitis (since (b)(6)), s/p gallbladder removal (b)(6) pre-diabetes controlled with diet (since (b)(6)), rheumatoid arthritis, systemic lupus erythematosus (since (b)(6)), migraines, and a history of recurrent VVC. The was randomized into the study and received the first dose of oteseconazole 150-mg x 12 weeks on Study Day 1. Final dose of oteseconazole received on Study Day 22 due to premature discontinuation of study treatment secondary to SAE (pancreatitis). The subject completed their participation in the study on Study Day 337.
L2177: On Study Day 25, the participant developed abdominal pain, nausea, emesis, lightheadedness, and collapsed to the floor. Subject presented to the ER and was hospitalized. A CT of the
L2178: abdomen and pelvis with contrast was obtained and was largely unremarkable. "At presentation, the subject's amylase was mildly increased at 141 IU/L (reference range 28-100 IU/L) and lipase was mildly increased at 58.0 U/L (reference range 10-55 U/L). The subject also tested positive for cannabinoids in the urine." On Study Day 26, the subject received an ultrasound of the abdomen that was normal except for a surgically removed gallbladder, "with presumed mild compensatory dilatation of the biliary system." The pancreas was partially visualized and grossly unremarkable. "An magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography showed no filling defect, choledocholithiasis, biliary stricture, or obstruction was noted."
L2179: Two days later, Study Day 28, an endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography with sphincterotomy was performed, and the subject was diagnosed with acute pancreatitis (Study Days 25-33). The subject was treated with various concomitant medications notably including hydromorphone, morphine sulfate, cefazolin, indomethacin, pancrelipase, sucralfate, esomeprazole, famotidine, and cholestyramine. Study drug treatment was discontinued on Study Day 22. Subject was discharged from the hospital with resolution of pancreatitis on Study Day 33. The Study Investigator determined that the acute episode of pancreatitis was unlikely related to oteseconazole exposure.
L2180: The subject experienced a moderate SAE of abdominal pain on Study Day 81 (Study Days 81-84) for which subject presented to the ER with an inability to tolerate any oral intake. The subject was hospitalized and underwent a work-up that was unrevealing. While hospitalized, the subject received hydromorphone, morphine, tramadol, pancrelipase, sucralfate, and pantoprazole. Subject was discharged home on Study Day 84 with reported resolution of abdominal pain.
L2181: *Study Investigator's/Applicant's Impression:* The Investigator assessed the two events as being unrelated to oteseconazole therapy.
L2182: **Clinical Reviewer's Assessment:** Clinical reviewer has reviewed accompanying data and subject narrative and agrees that the observed SAEs were most likely unrelated to oteseconazole exposure. These SAEs occurred in a subject with a known history of chronic pancreatitis and apparent chronic GI issues. In addition, the subject had multiple other co-morbid conditions. The initial case of pancreatitis demonstrated only mild lipase elevations with largely unremarkable imaging, and resulted in discontinuation of the study drug. The second SAE occurred following discontinuation of oteseconazole exposure and was unlikely related to oteseconazole administration.

L2184: ## Subject (b)(6) (Placebo) (PT: Paronychia)
L2185: A 27-year-old White, non-Latino female with a past medical history significant for obesity (BMI 37.1), depression (since (b)(6)), genital herpes (since (b)(6)), and a history of RVVC was randomized into Study CL-006 and received the first dose of placebo on Study 1. On Study Day 174, subject received the final dose of placebo and completed the study on Study Day 337.

On Study Day 301, the subject presented to primary physician's office febrile to 103 F and was instructed to return to the ER where the subject was noted to have
L2187: erythematous streaking of a left upper extremity cellulitis and was diagnosed with SAE (PT: herpetic cellulitis). The subject was discharged from the ER to home that same day only to develop worsening swelling, redness, discoloration and increased streaking that had spread to their forearm.
L2188: On Study Day 302, subject was admitted and treated with vancomycin, ibuprofen, and valacyclovir. L2189: *Study Investigator's/Applicant's Impression:* The Investigator assessed the event as being unrelated to receipt of placebo.

The subject quite possibly had a herpetic whitlow resulting in cellulitis and lymphangitis. Unclear if SAE was the result of auto-inoculation since there was no mention of an ongoing genital herpes outbreak, however, subject does have a known history of genital herpes.

L2477: ## 17.4. Creatine Phosphokinase Narratives
L2478: ## 17.4.1. CPK Elevations-Trial CL-011
L2479: ## **Subject** (b) (6)
L2480: ## **Grade 4 CPK Elevations \ge 20 \times ULN Study Day 45 of Oteseconazole Treatment**
L2481: A 50-year-old Black/African-American, non-Latino female with a prior medical history relevant for genital herpes for which the sole concomitant medication was valacyclovir as needed. Subject was observed to have a baseline CPK of 166 U/L (within normal limits [wnl]). On Study Day 45 of oteseconazole (OTE) treatment, the subject was observed to have a **Grade 4 CPK elevation** of 11,188 U/L (58x upper limit of normal [ULN]). This finding was associated with **Grade 3 AST elevations** (AST of 142 U/L, 4.6x ULN) and **Grade 1 ALT elevations** (ALT of 65 U/L,
L2482: 2.0x ULN) which were also witnessed to occur on Study Day 45. Study Day 45 creatinine (Cr) levels and subsequent Cr levels were unaffected by the reported CPK elevation, as were pre- and post- potassium (K^+), phosphate, and calcium (Ca^{2+}) levels. This event was the subject's sole reported TEAE (PT: blood creatine phosphokinase increased Study Day 45-56). The Study Investigator and Applicant believed this event to be unrelated to the study drug. The dose of oteseconazole was not changed and no further actions were taken. The patient completed study.
L2483: **Clinical Reviewer:** The observed CPK elevation lasted approximately 12 days (Study Day 45 - Study Day 56) and was temporally associated with on treatment oteseconazole exposure, although it did not require discontinuation of oteseconazole. There were no reported related TEAEs such as myopathy, muscle pain or weakness associated with the observed CPK elevations.
L2484: **Probably related:** Given the totality of CPK elevations observed throughout the oteseconazole Phase 1-3 development program coupled with the drug's long half-life and the temporal relatedness of the event to drug administration, the reviewer cannot exclude the possibility that the event is associated with oteseconazole exposure. Increased CPK elevations have also been reported to occur in other azoles—itraconazole in the post-market setting and voriconazole in &lt;2% of cases. A causal mechanism of action between the drug and the observed event has not been elucidated.
L2485: ## **Subject** (b) (6)
L2486: ## **Grade 4 CPK Elevations \ge 20 \times ULN, Study Day 140 (1.8 Months Post-OTE Exposure)**
L2487: A 33-year-old White, non-Latino female with no relevant past medical history was begun on oteseconazole on Study Day 1, with a baseline CPK value of 67 U/L (wnl; Grade 0). Final on-treatment CPK value (Study Day 88) was also within normal limits at 76 U/L. On Study Day 140, 53 days (1.8 months) after the end of treatment, the subject developed a transient **Grade 4** CPK elevation of 4166 U/L (21.7x ULN) (which spontaneously declined to 133 U/L on Study Day 151. Concomitant medications were all pre-treatment, including: cholestyramine (for diarrhea), multivitamins, and levonorgestrel oral contraceptive pill, and were unlikely culprits in the observed elevations. Subject's only TEAE (PT nausea) occurred on Study Days 3-5 of treatment. Coinciding with subject's Study Day 140 CPK elevations were a **Grade 2** AST elevation of 62 U/L (2x ULN) and a **Grade 1** ALT elevation of 46 U/L (1.4x ULN), both of which returned to baseline and pre-baseline levels shortly afterwards on Study Day 151 (AST of 13 U/L, ALT of 13 U/L), similar to what was observed with CPK elevations. Other related laboratories (e.g., Cr, K^+, phosphate, Ca^{2+}), pre-and post-event values remained within normal limits.
L2488: **Clinical Reviewer:** Transient temporal association not requiring study drug discontinuation. This event was not captured by the Study Investigator as a TEAE event. The Applicant reported being fairly confident that the observed CPK elevation was unrelated to oteseconazole treatment since the event was transient in nature. The Applicant provided no alternative explanations for the observed CPK findings (e.g., strenuous exercise). The participant had no documented associated TEAEs of myopathy, muscle pain, muscle weakness.
L2489: **Probably related:** Given the totality of CPK elevations observed throughout the oteseconazole Phase 1-3 development program coupled with the drug's long half-life, with the event occurring within the timeframe of the drug's first half-life (although the subject was no longer actively receiving the study day), the reviewer cannot exclude the possibility that the event is associated with oteseconazole exposure. Increased CPK elevations have also been reported to occur in azoles—itraconazole and voriconazole. A causal mechanism of action between the drug and the event has yet to be elucidated.
L2490: ## **Subject** (b) (6)
L2491: ## **Grade 4 CPK Elevations \ge 20 \times \text{ULN}, Study Day 202 (3.9 Months Post-OTE Exposure)**
L2492: A 38-year-old Asian, non-Latino female with no pertinent medical history (e.g., multiple episodes of bacterial vaginosis, seasonal allergies) had a baseline (Study Day 1) CPK value of 136 U/L (wnl). Final on-treatment CPK value was 100 (wnl) (Study Day 85). On Study Day 202, 118 days (3.9 months) post-treatment, the subject was reported to have a **Grade 4** CPK elevation of 4140 (21.5xULN). Subsequent Study Day 221, 252, and 287 CPK values were within normal limits (wnl), with values of 81, 101, and 97, respectively. Simultaneous Study Day 202 laboratory elevations included **Grade 3** AST (96 U/L, 3.1x ULN) and **Grade 1** ALT elevations (39, 1.2xULN). These laboratory elevations were also transient and returned to baseline values on subsequent study days. Other pertinent laboratories, Cr, K+, phosphate, and Ca2+, remained within normal limits pre- and post- baseline and were unaffected by the observed CPK elevations.
L2493: The concomitant medication administered around the time of the CPK elevations was the third generation cephalosporin cefcapene for (PT: cystitis) (Study Days 202 to 205). Simultaneous TEAEs occurring at or around the time of the witnessed CPK elevations included: (PT: blood triglycerides) (Study Days 177 to 183); (PT: cystitis) (Study Days 202 to 205); (PT: AST increased) (Study Days 202 to 221); and (PT: blood creatine phosphokinase increased) (Study Days 202 to 221). The Study Investigator and the Applicant deemed the reported laboratory elevations as being unrelated to oteseconazole administration and no additional actions were taken with oteseconazole.
L2494: **Clinical Reviewer:** CPK elevations and (not unexpectedly) transaminase elevations were transient and occurred 118 days (approximately 4 months) post-treatment and hence did not require study drug discontinuation. There were no reported TEAEs related to rhabdomyolysis such as myopathy or muscle pain or weakness. However, the PT: "AST increased" occurred concurrent with the CPK elevations since release of AST is associated with muscle breakdown. The subject also experienced the TEAE PT: cystitis which was treated with the Japanese third generation cephalosporin cefcapene whose side effect profile does not include CPK (but does on rare occasions affect hematologic parameters [e.g., agranulocytosis, thrombocytopenia, hemolytic anemia] and is associated with fulminant hepatitis and hepatic function disorder). The Applicant stated that they have a "high level of confidence" that the CPK elevations were not associated with oteseconazole. No causally related alternate explanations were offered.
L2495: **Possibly related:** Given the totality of CPK elevations observed throughout the oteseconazole Phase 1-3 development program coupled with the drug's long half-life, the reviewer cannot exclude the possibility that the observed CPK elevations are related to oteseconazole exposure.
L2496: *Increased CPK elevations have also been reported to occur in azoles—itraconazole and voriconazole.*

Increased CPK elevations have also been reported to occur in azoles—itraconazole and voriconazole. A causal mechanism of action between the drug and the event has yet to be elucidated.

L2608: ## 18. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance: Additional Information and Assessment
L2609: ## Mechanism of Action
L2610: Oteseconazole (OTE), VT-1161, is an azole metalloenzyme inhibitor targeting the fungal sterol, 14\alpha demethylase (CYP51), which mediates membrane permeability and fluidity by demethylating the 14\alpha position of lanosterol thereby affecting an early step in the biosynthesis of ergosterol. In a study comparing the binding affinity of clotrimazole, fluconazole, voriconazole, itraconazole and OTE to *C. albicans* CYP51 it was found that OTE has a greater affinity for the peptides that form the backbone of CYP51, than itraconazole and voriconazole (Hoekstra et al. 2014; Warriolow et al. 2014). Oteseconazole was shown to be unique with now detectable to the human cytochrome, HsCYP51, at the concentrations studied.
L2611: ## Resistance
L2612: Azole resistance in *Candida albicans* may be due to various resistant mechanisms such as efflux pump over expression, over expression of *CYP51*, mutations in *ERG11* or an alteration in ergosterol biosynthesis. To identify determinants of oteseconazole resistance, the influence of specific azole resistance mechanisms on oteseconazole, when placed in a fluconazole-susceptible strain, SC5314, was evaluated (Nishimoto et al. 2019). Increased *CDR1* and *CDR2* expression through the activation of the transcriptional activator of *CDR1* activation of the (*TAC1*) gene, a zinc cluster transcription factor (*ZCFs*) that regulates *CDR1* and *CDR2*, increased oteseconazole MICs &gt;8 fold compared to the susceptible parent strain suggesting that the overexpression of the *TAC1* transcription factor and *CDR1* is associated with increased oteseconazole MICs. Similar effects were observed with voriconazole which increased MICs 16-fold compared to the parent strain. Therefore, it appears that drug efflux via *CDR1* expression is associated with an increase in oteseconazole MIC.
L2613: In another experiment, the effect of a gain of function mutation in multidrug resistance regulator 1 (*Mrr1*), a ZCF that controls the expression of *MDR1*, a major facilitator transporter, that prevents drug accumulation in yeast cells, was examined. Mutations in *Mrr1* was associated with a 4-fold increase in oteseconazole MIC, suggesting that *MDR1* transporter is possibly involved in resistance. An increase in MIC values was also observed with fluconazole and voriconazole. In another experiment, the effects of different alterations in the azole target enzyme on *C. albicans* susceptibility to oteseconazole was investigated. Amino acid substitutions in the Egr11 (another ZCF) resulted in a 4-16-fold increases in oteseconazole MIC values.
L2614: ## Animal Studies
L2615: The efficacy of VT-1161 was evaluated in a number of animal models infected with *C. albicans* and *C. glabrata* but are not directly related to VVC or RVVC.
L2616: 1. In a Murine Estrogen-Dependent model, mice were administration estradiol and then infected with *C. albicans* ATCC 3153A into the vagina. Three days after infection, the mice were treated orally with 4, 10, and 25 mg/kg, of oteseconazole and 25 mg/kg fluconazole for 4 days and the fungal burden was measured in vaginal lavage solutions 1- and 4- days post-treatment. Each
L2617: oteseconazole dose, at both time points, was significantly superior to vehicle-treated control animals in suppressing vaginal fungal burden (p value &lt;0.0002). In comparing the 2 agents, oteseconazole and fluconazole, at the same time points, there was no significant difference. Both suppressed fungal burden by &gt;99\% compared with control, and both cleared the same percent of animals (\sim 70\%) of detectable fungus.
L2618: 2. In a neutropenic mouse candidiasis model, female CD-1 mice were inoculated by tail vein with 0.1 mL of 2.7\times 10^5 CFU/mL *C. albicans* R303 strain. VT-1161, terbinafine or no treatment (control) were administered at 2 hours after infection by oral gavage. The concentrations of VT-1161 were 5, 10 and 20 mg/kg and colony forming units (CFU) of homogenized kidneys were determined. A dose response was observed with VT-1161 with mean \log_{10} CFU/kidney ranging from 2.74 to 4.39 at 24 hours post-infection, compared to 5.10 \log_{10} CFU/kidney for the nontreated control. Mice treated with terbinafine had a fungal burden in the kidneys comparable to the nontreated control.
L2619: 3. The efficacy of VT-1161 was compared to amphotericin B, caspofungin and fluconazole in a mouse model of *Candida* kidney infection. A concentration of 0.1 mL of 1.22\times 10^5 CFU/mL was used to establish infection. Mice (10 per group) infected with *C. albicans* were administered VT-1161 (2, 5, 10 or 20 mg/kg) or fluconazole (2, 5, 10 or 20 mg/kg) orally once per day on days 1 through 4. All kidneys were collected at death or the end of study and the fungal burden was determined. Survival was measured up to 18 days post-infection. The median survival time in untreated animals was 5 days. VT-1161 exhibited a dose dependent survival benefit, mice treated with 10 mg/kg or higher had 100% survival. The median survival time for animals treated with 2 mg/kg VT-1161 was extended to 14 days. In comparison, mice treated with 2, 5, 10 and 20 mg/kg fluconazole had median survival times of 6.5, 11, 12.5 and &gt;18 days. All mice treated with 1 mg/kg amphotericin B or 0.25 mg/kg caspofungin survived.
L2620: 4. The efficacy of VT-1161 against *C. glabrata* was evaluated in a female CD-1 neutropenic, mouse candidiasis systemic infection model. The mice were infected with 0.1 mL of 7.5\times 10^6 CFU/mL *C. glabrata* strain R363. Treatment (5 or 20 mg/kg) was administered by tail vein injection 2 hours after inoculation according to body weight. Kidneys and blood were collected from four mice in each group on day 5 and all surviving mice in the VT-1161 treatment groups on day 19. Survival was assessed on Day 19. Nearly all mice survived when treated with any dose (5 or 20 mg/kg) of VT-1161; however, survival was dose dependent in mice treated with fluconazole. The MIC for VT-1161 against this strain was 0.06 mcg/mL against *C. glabrata* (n=10) wild type clinical isolates and 2 mcg/mL against 10 isolates characterized as azole resistant.

Refer to Section 21 for additional details.
