L2632: ## FULL PRESCRIBING INFORMATION
L2633: ## 1. INDICATIONS AND USAGE
L2634: ## 1.1 Vulvovaginal Candidiasis
L2635: The proposed indication [REDACTED] (b)(4) [REDACTED] (b)(4) was changed because the information provided by the Applicant was insufficient to support this indication. Based on the data submitted, VIVJOA (oteseconazole) is indicated to reduce the incidence of recurrent vulvovaginal candidiasis (RVVC) in females with a history of RVVC, who are not of reproductive potential. Refer to Section 1.2.2 of this document for additional details.
L2636: The change in the terminology [REDACTED] (b)(4) to “reduce the incidence of RVVC” is supported [REDACTED] (b)(4) [REDACTED] (b)(4)
L2637: ## 2. DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION
L2638: (b) (4)
L2639: An alternate fluconazole/VIVJOA dosage regimen was added because this was [REDACTED] (b) (4) Section II.6.1 of this review for additional details.
L2640: (b) (4)
L2641: [REDACTED] (b) (4) Retained instructions for administration of VIVJOA with food since this is how VIVJOA was studied in the clinical trials and there is uncertainty about efficacy under fasted conditions. Refer to Section II.8.2.1 of this review for additional details.
L2642: ## 4. CONTRAINDICATIONS
L2643: - Contraindications in females of reproductive potential and pregnant and lactating women were added based on the ocular abnormalities observed in animal studies and the prolonged drug exposure window of approximately 690 days (based on 5 times the half-life) of oteseconazole that precludes adequate risk mitigation of potential risks associated with VIVJOA use. Refer to Section II.7.6 of this review for additional details.
- A contraindication in patients with hypersensitivity was added based on the demonstrated cases of hypersensitivity observed with the use of VIVJOA in the clinical trials. Refer to Sections II.7.6.7; III.17.3.3 of this review for additional details.
L2644: ## 5. WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS
L2645: ## 5.1 Embryo-Fetal Toxicity
L2646: A warning and precaution describing the risk of embryo-fetal toxicity was added based on animal studies that indicated VIVJOA may cause fetal harm. A statement regarding the drug exposure window of approximately 690 days (based on 5 times the half-life of oteseconazole) precluding adequate mitigation of the embryo-fetal toxicity risks was added to the Warnings and Precautions and subsections 8.1 and 8.3 of the PI. Refer to Section I.2.2 and II.7.6 of this review for additional details.
L2647: ## 6. ADVERSE REACTIONS
L2648: Presentation of safety information including most frequently reported adverse reactions, and other less common adverse reactions from the three VIVJOA clinical trials was modified to highlight safety from the three trials separately due to the differences in the comparator groups, as opposed to the Applicant's proposal to display pooled safety adverse reactions from all three trials. Refer to Section II.7.6 of this review for additional details.
L2649: A commentary on the elevations in creatinine phosphokinase (CPK) that were observed in the patients treated with VIVJOA compared to the comparator groups was added. Refer to Sections II.7.6.6, II.7.7.2, and III.17.4 of this review for additional details.
L2650: A disclaimer statement was added to avoid implied use of VIVJOA in females of reproductive potential as follows: “Although females of reproductive potential were included in the clinical safety data, VIVJOA is contraindicated in females of reproductive potential because of a risk of embryo-fetal toxicity [see *Contraindications* (4), *Warnings and Precautions* (5.1), and *Use in Specific Populations* (8.1, 8.3)].”
L2651: ## 8. USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS
L2652: ## 8.1 Pregnancy, 8.2 Lactation and 8.3 Females of Reproductive Potential
L2653: Based on the benefit/risk analysis for oteseconazole, including the concerning ocular abnormalities observed in the animal studies, the inability to exclude the risk of cataracts in humans and the prolonged drug exposure window of approximately 690 days (based on 5 times the half-life) of oteseconazole, the contraindication statement for use in females of reproductive potential and pregnant women lactating women was added. Additional details on the ocular abnormalities observed in animal studies and the Agency's current definition of females who are not of reproductive potential were included in this section of the PI. Refer to Sections I.2.2 and II.8.4 of this review for additional details.
L2654: ## 8.4 Pediatric Use
L2655: A contraindication statement in females of reproductive potential was added. (b) (4)
(b) (4)
(b) (4) VIVJOA is not recommended for use in pre-menarchal pediatric patients. Refer to Section II.8.3 of this review for additional details.
L2656: ## 8.6 Renal Impairment
L2657: A statement on the use of VIVJOA in patients with renal impairment was added to indicate that no dosage adjustment is recommended in patients with mild to moderate renal impairment. However, VIVJOA is not recommended for use in patients with severe renal impairment or end-stage renal disease (ESRD) with or without dialysis. These labeling recommendations were based on pharmacokinetic analysis. Refer to Section II.8.1 of this review for additional details.
L2658: ## 8.7 Hepatic Impairment
L2659: A statement on the use of VIVJOA in patients with hepatic impairment was added to indicate that clinically relevant differences in the safety of VIVJOA between patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A) and the indicated population were not expected. However, VIVJOA is not recommended for use in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment due to insufficient information. These labeling recommendations were based on pharmacokinetic analysis. Refer to Section II.8.1 of this review for additional details.

L2663: ## 13. NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
L2664: ## 13.2 Animal Toxicology and/or Pharmacology
L2665: Data regarding the increased incidences of hemorrhage were added to highlight the possible effects of long-term exposure to high doses of oteseconazole. This information was added to this subsection because it was considered to be significant animal data necessary for the safe and effective use of VIVJOA in humans that is not incorporated in other sections of the PI as delineated in 21 CFR 201.57(14)(ii). Refer to Section 1.2.2 of this review for additional details.
L2666: ## 14 CLINICAL STUDIES
L2667: The clinical studies discussed in Section 14 of the PI included effectiveness and safety data for females of reproductive potential. A disclaimer statement was added to note that VIVJOA is contraindicated in females of reproductive potential to avoid implying the use of the VIVJOA in this population as delineated in 21 CFR 201.57(c)(15)(i) that emphasizes that any clinical study discussed in Section 14 must not imply or suggest indications or uses not stated in the Indications and Usage section of the PI. The addition of the disclaimer statement was based on the recommendations noted in section II.B of the guidance for industry: Clinical Studies Section of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products – Content and Format (January 2006) that indicates that if such studies are included in Section 14 of the PI, “the limitations of the study and the reasons for inclusion should be stated.” The disclaimer statement added to Section 14 was as follows: Although females of reproductive potential were included in the clinical efficacy data, VIVJOA is contraindicated in females of reproductive potential because of a risk of embryo-fetal toxicity [see *Contraindications (4), Warnings and Precautions (5.1), and Use in Specific Populations (8.1, 8.3)*].
L2668: The use of rescue VVC medication is an intercurrent event that confounds the assessment of a possible culture-verified recurrence at a later timepoint. Furthermore, the use of additional VVC medication was noted to be higher on the oteseconazole arm compared to placebo in subjects for who a culture verified episode was not reported. Ignoring the use of rescue medication overestimates the effect of treatment with oteseconazole. Therefore, the results of the sensitivity analysis that treats VVC medication use as an event were added for each trial. Refer to Section 6.3.1 for additional details.
L2669: (b) (4)
L2670: For Trial 017, it was expected that subjects who failed to resolve their infection at Day 14 would be considered events in the primary analysis. The results presented were revised based on this definition. Refer to Section 6.2.3.3 for additional details.
L2671: (b) (4)
L2672: (b) (4)

L2677: ## 22. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2678: The following Postmarketing Commitments were agreed to by the Applicant:
L2679: ## 4221-1: Conduct in Vitro Drug Interaction Studies Evaluating Oteseconazole as a Substrate or Inhibitor of MATEs (MATE1 and MATE2-K).
L2680: Final Report Submission date: July 2022.
L2681: The goal of this in vitro study is to evaluate the potential for MATE-transporter mediated drug-drug interactions with oteseconazole by determining whether oteseconazole is a substrate or inhibitor of this transporter. Data from this study will determine the need for dedicated clinical drug-drug studies with oteseconazole and MATE transporter substrates and inhibitors.
L2682: ## 4221-2: Complete the Five Ecotoxicity Studies That Are Currently in Progress and Submit the Results of the Assays.
L2683: Final Report Submission date: May 2022.
L2684: Five ecotoxicity study reports listed in Table 2 of the Mycovia response to the October 7, 2021, Information Request (IR) are needed to support the categorical exclusion from an environmental assessment (EA) in accordance with 21 CFR 25.31(b), which is for substances that increase in use but result in an expected introduction concentration (EIC) of &lt; 1 ppb.

L2749: ## 1. Background
L2750: In this submission, the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in regular rats and one in transgenic mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of VT-1161 in rats and mice when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for about 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice. Results of this review have been discussed with the reviewing pharmacologist Dr. McMaster.
L2751: In this review, the phrase "dose response relationship" (trend) refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases.
L2752: ## 2. Rat Study
L2753: In this study two separate experiments were conducted, one in male rats and one in female rats. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and eighty Sprague-Dawley rats of each sex were assigned to three treated groups and one vehicle control group by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 70 animals, as indicated in Table 1. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 0.5, 1.5, 5 mg/kg/day for males and females, for up to 104 weeks. In this review, these dose groups were referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The vehicle control group received the vehicle [0.5% (w/w) Carboxymethylcellulose (CMC: medium viscosity) in deionized water], administered orally by gavage for about 104 weeks in the same manner as the treated groups. Both male and female rats in the 5 mg/kg/day (high) dose group had similar clinical findings and body weight trends; therefore, the dose level was decreased from 5mg/kg/day to 3 mg/kg/day during Week 55 for males and week 33 for females. Starting Day 478 (Week 69), the high dose males were placed on a 6-week dosing holiday, dosing resumed on Day 520 (Week 75) and that group (high) was terminated early during Week 77 due to survival reaching 20 animals in the group. All other male groups were terminated during Week 90 due to survival in the vehicle control group reaching 20 animals. Also, in female rats, due to early termination criteria, the study was terminated early based on high mortality/low survivability, animal in group 1, 3, and 4 were terminated during Week 86, groups 2 were terminated during Week 84.
L2754: The change of dosing was not taken into consideration by the sponsor for determination of a weighted dose to be used for the group in the statistical analysis.
L2755: **Table 1: Experimental Design in Rat Study**
L2756: Group Name	Group No.	Dose Level (mg/kg/day)	Number of Animal
Male	Female	Males	Females
Vehicle Control	1	0	0	70	70
Low	2	0.5	0.5	70	70
Medium	3	1.5	1.5	70	70
High	4	5/3#	5/3#	70	70
L2757: Males in group 1, 2, and 3 were terminated during Week 90, groups 4 were terminated during Week 77. Females in group 1, 3, and 4 were terminated during Week 86, groups 2 were terminated during Week 84.
L2758: #: Dose reduced from 5 mg/kg/day to 3 mg/kg/day began on Day 379 (Week 55) for males and on Day 225 (Week 33) For females.
L2759: During the administration period, all animals were checked for morbidity, mortality, injury, and the availability of food and water twice daily, once in the morning and once in the afternoon. The animals were removed from the cage, and detailed observations were conducted for each animal prior to randomization on Day -2 and for each surviving main study animal weekly during the study. On occasion,
L2760: Clinical observations were recorded at unscheduled intervals. Detailed examinations for palpable masses were done weekly, the time of onset, location, size, appearance, and progression of each grossly visible or palpable mass, observed in carcinogenicity rats, was recorded weekly any animal showing signs of severe debility or intoxication, and if determined to be moribund or suffering excessively will be euthanized. Observations included, but were not limited to, evaluation of the skin, fur, eyes, ears, nose, oral cavity, thorax, abdomen, external genitalia, limbs and feet, respiratory and circulatory effects, autonomic effects such as salivation, nervous system effects including tremors, convulsions, reactivity to handling, and unusual behavior, and the palpation of masses. Histopathological examinations were performed on all animals found dead or killed moribund or sacrificed at the end of the experiment. Body weights for all animals were measured and recorded at receipt, prior to randomization and once weekly for the first 14 weeks, every two weeks until Week 28, every four weeks thereafter for the duration of the study, and on the day of the scheduled terminal necropsy.
L2761: ## 2.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2762: ## 2.1.1. Survival analysis
L2763: The Kaplan-Meier's curves were presented graphically for male and female rats separately. An overall test for survival was used to compare the homogeneity of survival rates across the groups using a log-rank test at the 0.05 significance level. If the survival rates were significantly different (p &lt; 0.05), then a follow up analysis was done where each treatment group was compared to vehicle control group using a log-rank test.
L2764: Any animal with accidental injury that causes its death, or its unscheduled sacrifice was censored in the estimation. In addition, all animals still alive at the end of the experimental period were censored at the following day. Results of all pair-wise comparisons are reported at the 0.05 and 0.01 significance levels. All endpoints were analyzed using two-tailed tests.
L2765: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2766: Sponsor's analysis showed the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 20 (28.6%), 19 (27.1%), 16 (22.8%), and 20 (28.6%) in the vehicle control group, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male rats, respectively, and 19 (27.1%), 20 (28.6%), 32 (45.7%), and 25 (35.7%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in female rats, respectively. The sponsor's report concluded that, there was a statistically significant difference in mortality between the high dose groups and the vehicle control group in male rats p &lt; 0.01.
L2767: ## 2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2768: Tumor incidence data were analyzed within each sex, using both survival adjusted and unadjusted tests. The unadjusted tests were based on the incidence and number of sites examined for each tumor type. The Cochran-Armitage trend test was calculated, and Fisher's exact test was used to compare each treatment group with the control groups. The Cochran-Armitage trend test was applied for all control and treatment groups and was arranged in a strictly increasing dose level. The survival adjusted test was conducted according to the prevalence/mortality methods described by Peto et al. The cut-off points for the interval-based test were Weeks 0 to 52, 53 to 78, 79 to before terminal sacrifice, and the terminal sacrifice. Actual dose levels were used as the scores. All tumors in the scheduled terminal sacrifice interval were considered incidental for the purpose of statistical analysis.
L2769: The Poly-3 method was also used to assess prevalence of tumors. The survival-adjusted rates based on the risk weights were displayed. The tests of significance included both an overall trend and pair-wise comparisons of each treatment group with the corresponding control. All p-values are reported using upper-tailed tests.
L2770: ## Adjustment for the multiplicity:
L2771: For multiplicity testing adjustment, the sponsor used significance levels of 0.005 and 0.025 for common and rare tumors, respectively in dose response relationship (trend) tests and significance levels of 0.01 and 0.05 for common and rare tumors, respectively in pairwise comparisons. Site-specific background historical control database was used to determine whether the tumors should be designated as rare or common.
L2772: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2773: Following the multiple testing adjustment method described above, the sponsor's analysis showed, statistically significant VT-1161-related neoplastic findings occurred in the thyroid gland, liver, testes, and uterus.
L2774: Neoplastic findings in the thyroid gland consisted of benign follicular cell adenoma in both sexes at \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day and malignant follicular cell carcinoma in the thyroid gland in males at \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day and females at 5/3 mg/kg/day. The neoplastic findings in the liver consisted of an increased incidence, of benign hepatocellular adenomas in males at \ge 0.5 mg/kg/day and malignant hepatocellular carcinomas in females at 5/3 mg/kg/day. The neoplastic findings in the testes consisted of a statistically significant increased incidence of benign Leydig (interstitial) cell adenomas at \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day. And the neoplastic findings in the uterus consisted of an increased incidence of benign granular cell tumors at 5/3 mg/kg/day.

L2776: To verify sponsor's analysis and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the sponsor electronically on December 13, 2018 via SN0001.

L2779: ## 2.2.1 Survival analysis
L2780: In the reviewer's analysis, intercurrent mortality data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The Kaplan-Meier's curves were presented graphically for male and female rats separately. The
L2781: dose response relationship and homogeneity of survival distributions were tested for the treatment groups using the Likelihood Ratio test and the Log-Rank test. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rate are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2782: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2783: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 20 (28.6%), 19 (27.1%), 16 (22.8%), and 20 (28.6%) in the vehicle control group, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male rats, respectively, and 19 (27.1%), 20 (28.6%), 32 (45.7%), and 25 (35.7%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in female rats, respectively. This reviewer's analysis showed a statistically significant increase in mortality across the vehicle control group and the three treated groups in male rats (p&lt;0.0001). The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increase in mortality between the high dose groups and the vehicle control group in male rats (p=0.0016). However, in female rats, the pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant decrease in mortality between the medium dose group and the vehicle control group (p= 0.0185).
L2784: ## 2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2785: In the reviewer's analysis, the tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationship across vehicle control group and the treated groups, as well as the pairwise comparisons of vehicle control group with each of the treated groups using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993). In this method, an animal that lives the full study period (w_{\max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice with development of the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h=1. An animal that dies at Week w_h without development of the given tumor type before the end of the study gets a score of
L2786: s_h = \left( \frac{w_h}{w_{\max}} \right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \Sigma s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score
L2787: s_h=1 can be considered as a whole animal, while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \Sigma s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal develops the given tumor being tested, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise comparison) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k. For long term 104-week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature [Gebregziabher and Hoel (2009), Moon et al. (2003), Portier, et al. (1986)]. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of the data. Based on the intent to treat (ITT) principle Wmax was considered as 104 for both male and female rats.
L2788: For the calculation of p-values, if there were less than 10 tumor bearing animals across all treatment groups for a given tumor type, the exact tests based on the discrete permutation distribution were used, with dose levels (0, 5, 15, and 42 for male rats and 0, 5, 15, and 38 for female rats) as scores, and asymptotic tests were used for tumor types with higher incidences. The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male rats and female rats, respectively.
L2789: ## **Multiple testing adjustments:**
L2790: Following the FDA more recently revised draft guidance for the carcinogenicity study design and data analysis 2015, for the two-year rat study this reviewer used significance levels of 0.005 and 0.025 for common and rare tumors, respectively in dose response relationship (trend) tests and significance levels of 0.01 and 0.05 for common and rare tumors, respectively in pairwise comparisons.
L2791: A tumor is defined as a rare tumor if the published spontaneous rate or the spontaneous rate of the vehicle control of the tumor is less than 1%, and a common tumor is defined as one with tumor rate greater than or equal to 1%.

L2801: ## Reviewer's comment on a minor discrepancy between the SEND data set and the .xpt data set of the male rat experiment
L2802: *Animal 4059 in the high dose group of male rats was recorded as dying accidentally "ACCIDENTAL DEATH", at week 78 in the DS domain of the SEND data, which means DTHSACST = 4 (Accidental death) based on the format of the tumor.xpt. However, in the XTP data, it was recorded as a natural death, and was labelled as "DTHSACST = 1". However, this minor discrepancy between the two datasets did not affect the results of reviewer's survival and tumor data analyses.*
L2803: ## 3. Mouse Study
L2804: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in male mice and one in female mice. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred and fifteen rasH2 transgenic mice of each sex were assigned randomly to one of the five groups by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 25 animals except the positive control group which had 15 animals. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 0.5, 1.5, and 5 mg/kg/day for males and 1.5, 5, and 15 mg/kg/day for females, for up to 26 weeks, as indicated in Table 1. In this review, these dose groups would be referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The positive control group was administered with one intraperitoneal dose of 75 mg/kg NMU, N-methyl-N-nitrosourea on Day 1 of the dosing phase. This group was included to verify sensitivity of the test system to detect carcinogenicity effect. The vehicle control group received daily oral vehicle control article only [0.5% (w/w) Carboxymethylcellulose (CMC: medium viscosity) in deionized water.], were administered in the same manner as the treated groups.

L2809: During the administration period, all animals were checked for morbidity, mortality, injury, and availability of food and water, twice daily (a.m. and p.m.), abnormal findings were recorded for toxicity animals as they were observed. Cage side observations were conducted for each carcinogenicity animal at least twice daily, except on days when detailed observations were conducted. Abnormal findings or an indication of normal was recorded, any animal showing signs of severe debility or intoxication, and if determined to be moribund or suffering excessively was euthanized. Observations will include, but will not be limited to, evaluation of the skin, fur, eyes, ears, nose, oral cavity, thorax, abdomen, external genitalia, limbs and feet, respiratory and circulatory effects, autonomic effects such as salivation, nervous system effects including tremors, convulsions, reactivity to handling, unusual behavior, and palpation of tissue masses. Histopathological examinations were performed on all animals found dead or killed moribund or sacrificed at the end of the experiment. Body weights were recorded Within three days of arrival, at least once prior to randomization and/or Day -1, and once weekly for the first 14 weeks, every two weeks thereafter.

L2812: Intercurrent mortality data will be analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method. An overall test comparing all groups were conducted using a log-rank test. If this overall test was significant (p&lt;0.05) and there were more than two groups, then a follow up analysis will be done where each treatment group will be compared to the control group using a log-rank test. Results of all pair-wise comparisons will be reported at the 0.05 and 0.01 significance levels. All endpoints will be analyzed using two-tailed tests.

L2836: ## 4. Summary
L2837: In this submission, the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in regular rats and one in transgenic mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of VT-1161 in rats and mice when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for about 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice.
L2838: ## **Rat Study:**
L2839: In this study two separate experiments were conducted, one in male rats and one in female rats. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and eighty Sprague-Dawley rats of each sex were assigned to three treated groups and one vehicle control group by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 70 animals, as indicated in Table 1. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 0.5, 1.5, 5 mg/kg/day for males and females, for up to 104 weeks. In this review, these dose groups were referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The vehicle control group received the vehicle [0.5% (w/w) Carboxymethylcellulose (CMC: medium viscosity) in deionized water], administered orally by gavage for about 104 weeks in the same manner as the treated groups. Both male and female rats in the 5 mg/kg/day (high) dose group had similar clinical findings and body weight trends; therefore, the dose level was decreased from 5mg/kg/day to 3 mg/kg/day during Week 55 for males and week 33 for females. Starting Day 478 (Week 69), the high dose males were placed on a 6-week dosing holiday, dosing resumed on Day 520 (Week 75) and that group (high) was terminated early during Week 77 due to survival reaching 20 animals in the group. All other male groups were terminated during Week 90 due to survival in the vehicle control group reaching 20 animals. Also, in female rats, due to early termination criteria, the study was terminated early based on high mortality/low survivability, animal in group 1, 3, and 4 were terminated during Week 86, groups 2 were terminated during Week 84.
L2840: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 20 (28.6%),
L2841: 19 (27.1%), 16 (22.8%), and 20 (28.6%) in the vehicle control group, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male rats, respectively, and 19 (27.1%), 20 (28.6%), 32 (45.7%), and 25 (35.7%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in female rats, respectively. This reviewer's analysis showed a statistically significant increase in mortality across the vehicle control group and the three treated groups in male rats (p&lt;0.0001). The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increase in mortality between the high dose groups and the vehicle control group in male rats (p=0.0016). However, in female rats, the pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant decrease in mortality between the medium dose group and the vehicle control group (p=0.0185).
L2842: For tumor data, following the multiple testing adjustment method described above, this reviewer's analysis showed statistically significant increasing dose response relationships across the vehicle control and the treated groups for the incidence of benign adenoma leydig cell in Testes, of benign adenoma, follicular Cell, and of the combined benign adenoma, follicular Cell and malignant carcinoma follicular Cell, in thyroid gland of male rats (p-value &lt;0.0001, &lt;0.0001, and &lt;0.0001, respectively). The pairwise comparisons test showed statistically significant increase in tumor incidences of benign adenoma leydig cell in Testes, of benign adenoma, follicular cell, and of the combined benign adenoma, follicular cell and malignant carcinoma follicular cell, in thyroid gland in medium and high dose group, when compare to the vehicle control group in male rats (p-value=0.0033, &lt;0.0001, &lt;0.0001, and &lt;0.0001, &lt;0.0001, &lt;0.0001, respectively). Also, the pairwise comparisons test showed statistically significant increase in tumor incidences of benign adenoma, Hepatocellular in liver, and malignant carcinoma follicular Cell, in thyroid gland in the medium dose group, when compare to the vehicle control group in male rats (p-value=0.0053, and =0.0014, respectively).
L2843: In female rats, this reviewer's analysis showed statistically significant increasing dose response relationships across the vehicle control and the treated groups for the incidence of malignant carcinoma, hepatocellular in the liver, of benign adenoma, follicular Cell, malignant carcinoma follicular cell and of the combined benign adenoma, follicular Cell and malignant carcinoma follicular cell, in thyroid gland, and, of benign granular cell tumor, in the uterus with cervix (p=0.0169, &lt;0.0001, =0.0010, &lt;0.0001, and &lt;0.0018, respectively). The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in the medium and high doses group for the incidences of benign adenoma, follicular Cell, and of the combined benign adenoma, follicular cell and malignant carcinoma follicular cell, in thyroid gland, when compared to the vehicle control group in female rats (p=0.0007, 0.0003 and &lt;0.0001, &lt;0.0001, respectively). Also, the pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant increase in the high dose group for the incidence of malignant carcinoma follicular cell in the thyroid gland, when compared to the vehicle control group (p=0.0224), as indicated in Table 2.
L2844: No other significant dose response relationship or pairwise comparisons were noted in either sex.
L2845: ## Mouse Study:
L2846: In this study two separate experiments were conducted, one in male mice and one in female mice. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred and fifteen rasH2 transgenic mice of each sex were assigned randomly to one of the five groups by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 25 animals except the positive control group which had 15 animals. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 0.5, 1.5, and 5 mg/kg/day for males and 1.5, 5, and 15 mg/kg/day for females, for up to 26 weeks, as indicated in Table 1. In this review, these dose groups would be referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The positive control group was administered with one intraperitoneal dose of 75 mg/kg NMU, N-methyl-N-nitrosourea on Day 1 of the dosing phase. This group was included to verify
L2847: sensitivity of the test system to detect carcinogenicity effect. The vehicle control group received daily oral vehicle control article only [0.5% (w/w) Carboxymethylcellulose (CMC: medium viscosity) in deionized water.], were administered in the same manner as the treated groups.
L2848: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (96%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 23 (92%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male mice, respectively, and 23 (92%), 24 (96%), 25 (100%), and 23 (92%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in females, respectively. The reviewer's report showed no statistically significant dose response relationship in the mortality of both male and female mice. The pairwise comparisons also showed no statistically significant increase or decrease in mortality between the treated groups, and the vehicle control group in either sex of mice.
L2849: For tumor data, following the multiple testing adjustment method described above, this reviewer's analysis showed no tumor types with a statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidences with increased VT-1161 dose in both male and female mice. The pairwise comparisons test also showed no statistically significant increases in tumor incidences in any groups, when compare to the vehicle control group in both male and female mice.
L2850: The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in the positive control group for the incidences of malignant lymphoma in multicentric neoplasm, of benign papilloma, in skin, and of benign papilloma, and of benign papilloma in stomach, when compared to the vehicle control group in male mice (p-values =0.0431, =0.0040, and &lt;0.0001, respectively). Also, the pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in the positive control group for the incidences of benign adenoma, in harderian glands, benign papilloma, in skin, benign papilloma, in stomach, and of benign polyp, endometrial stromal, in uterus with cervix, in female mice (p-values =0.0468, =0.0018, &lt;0.0001, and =0.0060, respectively).

L2910: ## **PHARMACOLOGY/TOXICOLOGY CONSULT ASSESSMENT AND EVALUATION**
L2911: Application Number*: NDA 215888 (IND 111675)
L2912: Sponsor: Mycovia Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
L2913: Product: VIVOJA (oteseconazole), capsule,
150 mg
L2914: Therapeutic area: Antifungal Agents- Systemic
L2915: Review Division: DAI
L2916: Reviewer: Lori Kotch, PhD, DABT
L2917: Division Director: Mukesh Summan, PhD
L2918: OID/DAI Contact: Gregory DiBernardo, 301-796-4063
L2919: Purpose of Review: Other
L2920: Consult Review
L2921: Requested Completion Date: October 1, 2021
L2922: ## Executive Summary (Clinical Relevance)
L2923: ## Consult Questions/ Responses
L2924: The PharmTox team in DAI request DPT-ORPURM input for the following questions related to cataract findings in PPND studies:
L2925: 1) These findings will be described in the prescribing information. Based on the presence of the cataracts, which appear to develop in multiple rat strains after PND 21, do you recommend additional studies to evaluate the risk of prenatal or neonatal exposure to oteseconazole to the offspring of patients? If so, what study design or additional ocular findings merit further evaluation (e.g., vision and visual acuity testing, etc.)?
L2926: *DPT-ORPURM Response: The multiple reproductive toxicity studies provided are adequate to characterize risk; I do not recommend additional studies. I am not aware of an accepted method to assess vision/visual acuity in animal models. However, given the toxicity (cataracts) one can presume effects to vision, the extent to which would be determined by severity of toxicity.*
L2927: 2) Have you previously observed similar ocular findings in pre- and post- natal, nonclinical animal studies, and if so, can you comment on the reversibility of these findings and what actions were taken based on the findings?
L2928: *DPT-ORPURM Response: I have not observed cataracts in previous PPND studies in this Division. Cataracts are generally not reversible.*
L2929: *Please note that cataracts would not be detected in an EFD study. Eyelids are closed at/around time of birth (i.e. no clinical observations) and sectioning methods (i.e. Bouins fixation, razor sectioning) preclude cataract determination. Skeletal processing methods remove all soft tissues and examination of ocular tissues is not possible in fetuses assigned to skeletal exam.*
L2930: 3) Based on these findings and your prior observations, would there be heightened potential for the described findings to occur in humans, particularly when considering the exposure at the NOAEL (1 mg/kg associated with an AUC of 39,360 ng*hr/mL) (in Pre- Post-natal study # 12344001) was about 0.5 times the clinical exposure (as well as the drug's long t1/2)?
L2931: *DPT-ORPURM Response: Treatment-related cataracts were produced in multiple reproductive toxicity studies, using multiple strains. Based on risk*
L2932: *estimate (0.5 times clinical exposure at NOAEL), it is reasonable to be concerned that such findings may occur in patients at clinical dose. The long half-life of the drug further elevates concern.*
L2933: ## **Relevant Background Provided by Requesting Division:**
L2934: On May 27, 2021, a type 1, NME for oteseconazole was submitted under NDA 215888 for FDA review. This product has QIDP and Fast Track designations and will be reviewed as a Priority NDA under the "Program." We currently do not plan to hold an Advisory Committee meeting for this NDA.
L2935: 5/27/21 EDR Location: \CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDA215888\0001
L2936: In Pre- Post-natal study 12344001, oral administration of oteseconazole to pregnant SD rats at 0, 1, 3 and 7.5 mg/kg/day once daily from Gestation Day 6 through Lactation Day 20 resulted in eye opacities at doses as low as 3 mg/kg. Opacities were observed (during clinical evaluations) as early as Day 21 (but not on Day 17). Some animals only developed cataracts/opacities beginning Day 49 or 56 or 84. The development of these opacities/cataracts was confirmed in three subsequent studies (#1234002, 1234003 and 1234004), where only the 7.5 mg/kg dose was used and ocular examinations were conducted by a board-certified veterinary ophthalmologist in both preweaned and weaned rat pups (generally PND 17–22 and PND 30–36) using an indirect ophthalmoscope and slit lamp biomicroscope. Assuming clinical exposure of 85,000 ng*hr/mL, the exposure at the NOAEL (1 mg/kg associated with an AUC of 39,360 ng*hr/mL) was about 0.5 times the clinical exposure for patients being treated for acute vulvovaginal candidiasis.

L2940: In pre-postnatal study #1234004, 7.5 mg/kg oteseconazole was administered to two strains of rats, (Sprague Dawley (SD) and Wistar Han (HAN)). A high incidence of lens opacities/cataracts was observed in the 7.5 mg/kg/day groups during the preweaning evaluation on PND 17–22, (11 and 32 pups for SD and HAN, respectively) which were seen for only a single female in the
L2941: SD control group and not seen in the HAN control group. The higher incidence of lens cataract was also observed at PND 30–36 (30 and 27 animals for SD and HAN, respectively) when compared to the respective control groups (7 and 0 for SD and HAN, respectively). No cataract/opacity was reported during the embryo-fetal development study of oteseconazole. Half of the fetuses were processed for skeletal examination while half were fixed in Bouin's solution and examined for soft tissue defects using the Wilson razor-blade sectioning technique.
L2942: ## **We seek your input on the following questions:**
L2943: - These findings will be described in the prescribing information. Based on the presence of the cataracts, which appear to develop in multiple rat strains after PND 21, do you recommend additional studies to evaluate the risk of prenatal or neonatal exposure to oteseconazole to the offspring of patients? If so, what study design or additional ocular findings merit further evaluation (e.g., vision and visual acuity testing, etc.)?
- Have you previously observed similar ocular findings in pre- and post- natal, nonclinical animal studies, and if so, can you comment on the reversibility of these findings and what actions were taken based on the findings?
- Based on these findings and your prior observations, would there be heightened potential for the described findings to occur in humans, particularly when considering the exposure at the NOAEL (1 mg/kg associated with an AUC of 39,360 ng*hr/mL) (in Pre- Post-natal study # 12344001) was about 0.5 times the clinical exposure (as well as the drug's long t_{1/2})?

L2952: Scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis (0 to 3) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis (0 to 16). The plot is divided into three regions: Cholestasis (Bilirubin >= 2x ULN), Potential Hy's Law (Bilirubin >= 2x ULN and ALT/AST >= 3x ULN), and Temple's Corollary (Bilirubin < 2x ULN and ALT/AST >= 3x ULN). Data points are categorized by trial (OTE Trial 017, OTE Trial 011, OTE Trial 012, OTE Trial 006, PLB Trial 017, PLB Trial 011, PLB Trial 012, PLB Trial 006) and pooled phase 3 results (Total OTE Phase 3, Total PLB Phase 3).
L2953: Box plot showing Apparent clearance (L/h) versus Renal function (CKD stages). The Y-axis ranges from 0.00 to 1.00 L/h. The X-axis shows four categories: Normal (n=438), Mild (n=404), Mild/moderate (n=36), and Moderate/severe (n=2). The median apparent clearance is approximately 0.25 L/h across all categories. The interquartile range (IQR) is roughly 0.15 to 0.35 L/h for Normal, Mild, and Mild/moderate categories. The Moderate/severe category shows a very low median clearance (around 0.1 L/h) and a very narrow IQR (around 0.05 to 0.15 L/h).
L2954: Scatter plot showing Vaginal CFU/100 µl lavage fluid (log scale) versus treatment groups (Vehicle, VT-1161 4 mg/kg, VT-1161 10 mg/kg, VT-1161 25 mg/kg, flu 25 mg/kg) on Day 7 and Day 10. Day 7 shows VT-1161 25 mg/kg significantly reducing CFU compared to Vehicle. Day 10 shows VT-1161 25 mg/kg significantly reducing CFU compared to Vehicle and flu 25 mg/kg.
L2955: Scatter plot showing Vaginal CFU (colony forming units) measured on Days 7 and 10 in a murine model of vaginal candidiasis following inoculation with Isolate AR466-06 (Left) and Isolate JJ330-06 (Right). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 10^1 to 10^6 CFU/100 µl lavage fluid. The X-axis shows treatment groups: Vehicle, Fluconazole, and VT-1161, for both Day 7 and Day 10. Isolate AR466-06 is represented by circles, and Isolate JJ330-06 is represented by triangles. For both isolates, the Vehicle group shows the highest CFU counts, while Fluconazole and VT-1161 show significantly lower CFU counts, indicating reduced mycologic eradication.
L2956: Scatter plot showing Vaginal CFU (colony forming units) measured on Days 7 and 10 in a murine model of vaginal candidiasis. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 10^1 to 10^8 CFU/100 µl lavage fluid. The X-axis shows Day 7 and Day 10 for two isolates: AR466-06 (Left) and LP1158-07 (Right). For both isolates, CFU levels are significantly lower in the Oteseconazole (VT-1161) group compared to the Vehicle and Fluconazole groups, especially on Day 10.
L2957: Figure 10: Mean Oteseconazole Plasma Concentration-Time Plots (20 – 320 mg). The graph shows Mean VT-1161 concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis versus Time (h) on the X-axis. Five dose levels are plotted: 20 mg (dashed line with triangles), 40 mg (solid line with diamonds), 80 mg (dotted line with asterisks), 160 mg (dash-dot line with squares), and 320 mg (solid line with stars). All doses show a rapid peak concentration followed by a slow decline. The 320 mg dose exhibits the highest peak concentration and the slowest decline, reaching approximately 100 ng/mL at 672 hours.
L2958: Figure 11 shows the Mean Oteseconazole Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles at Day 7 for four cohorts receiving different doses (40 mg, 80 mg, 160 mg, and 320 mg). The Y-axis represents Mean VT-1161 concentration in ng/mL (ranging from 0 to 4000), and the X-axis represents Time in hours (ranging from 0 to 24). The 40 mg dose (yellow circles) shows the lowest concentration, peaking around 400 ng/mL. The 80 mg dose (blue triangles) peaks around 1000 ng/mL. The 160 mg dose (red crosses) peaks around 1800 ng/mL. The 320 mg dose (black diamonds) shows the highest concentration, peaking around 3200 ng/mL. All profiles show a rapid increase in concentration, followed by a plateau phase, and then a gradual decline.
L2959: Figure 12 is a line graph illustrating Mean Digoxin Plasma Concentrations (ng/mL) over time (Hours from Dosing) following the administration of Digoxin Alone or Coadministration with Single or Multiple Doses of Oteseconazole (CL-015).
The X-axis represents Hours from Dosing (0 to 26). The Y-axis represents Plasma Digoxin Concentration (ng/mL) (0.0 to 1.8).
The legend identifies four conditions:
SD 0.5 mg digoxin Alone (Day 1) (Solid line with squares)
SD 150 mg VT-1161 + SD 0.5 mg digoxin (Day 3) (Dashed line with circles)
MD 150 mg VT-1161 + SD 0.5 mg digoxin (Day 16) (Dotted line with diamonds)
The graph shows that the peak concentration (C_{max}) and the area under the curve (AUC) are significantly higher when digoxin is coadministered with oteseconazole (Day 3 and Day 16) compared to administration alone (Day 1). The Day 3 data were baseline adjusted due to measurable pre-dose plasma concentrations.
L2960: Figure 13 displays four plots related to the Goodness of Fit (GOF) analysis of the final Population Pharmacokinetic (popPK) model. The top row shows observed concentrations versus predictions. The left plot shows Observations versus Population predictions, and the right plot shows Observations versus Individual predictions. Both plots show data points scattered around the identity line (red line). The bottom row shows residuals versus time or predictions. The left plot shows the absolute value of Individual Weighted Residuals (|IWRES|) versus Individual predictions, and the right plot shows Conditional weighted residuals versus Time. Both residual plots show data points scattered randomly around zero, indicating no systematic bias or major model misspecifications.
L2961: Figure 14: Visual Predictive Check (VPC) for the Final PK Model. The plot shows observed concentration data (blue dots) and predicted concentrations (red dashed line) over time (hours, 0 to 10,000). The Y-axis is Concentration (0 to 30,000). The plot includes shaded areas representing the 5th, 50th, and 95th percentiles of the predicted distribution, indicating the model's predictive performance.
L2962: Figure 15 displays six box plots showing predicted Oteseconazole pharmacokinetic parameters stratified by body weight (30 kg to 100 kg) for Adolescent and Adults With Recurrent Vulvovaginal Candidiasis under the proposed regimen. The parameters shown are AUC(0-Day1), AUC(Day1-Day14), AUC(Day14-Week3), AUC(Week13-Week14), C_{max} (Day 1, Day 2, Day 14, Week 13), and C_{min} (Day 1, Day 2, Day 14, Week 13). The Y-axis for AUC is \text{mg} \cdot \text{h}/\text{L} and for C_{max} is \text{mg}/\text{L}. The X-axis for all plots is Weight (kg). The number of subjects (n) is indicated for each weight stratum.
The plots show that AUC and C_{max} generally increase with body weight, while C_{min} appears relatively stable across weight strata.
